(6) **global client/contact custom fields Owner-only; an Admin adds or archives fields scoped to one of their assigned clients only; project/item Owner-only.** (7) required custom fields checked only by the saving form; never block a transition. (8) archived definitions keep values, hidden from forms, read-only under "Archived fields". (9) a field's type is immutable once a value exists; select stores the option key. (10) 3.2 ships the engine + Settings for client and contact; task and per-type scoping with 4.1. (11) logos ≤ 5 MB PNG/JPEG/WebP/SVG (sanitised), avatars ≤ 5 MB no SVG; original kept, browser-made JPEG preview. (12) previews through `/api/files/<id>` (permission-checked, private cache); presigned GET for downloads; buckets private. (13) replacing archives the old file; `storage_cleanup` deletes objects archived 30 days ago and `pending` older than 24 h; first Worker cron trigger → `/api/cron/storage-cleanup` with `CRON_SECRET`. (14) **MinIO in Docker for local dev and every e2e run (local and CI), same S3 API; R2 only on deployed staging and production.** Owner sets up R2 (buckets, token, CORS incl. preview origins) and the GitHub environment secrets/variables `R2_ACCOUNT_ID`, `R2_BUCKET`, `R2_ACCESS_KEY_ID`, `R2_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY`, `CRON_SECRET` (names only here). (15) `/people/[id]` is the person page for every member: Profile tab always, Attendance once joined and not the Owner; the Owner's own row on /me. (16) `team.view` opens the Profile tab; Attendance stays `attendance.view_all`. (17) Paused/Inactive clients stay fully editable, with a state banner. (18) client list: cards, search, state and Admin filters as view controls, Owner default Active (an Admin's too since the 3B review), ⋯ lifecycle actions.
- **Scope decision (2026-09-27, owner): five tasks are deferred past the launch** to reach a production-ready prototype sooner: **8.3** `core/drive`, **8.4b** (the Drive half of link submissions: access check, "Link is private", re-check, submission retention), **9.6** activity-history search, **9.5** exports, **10.1** global search, in that order in ROADMAP "Deferred past the launch". The pilot keeps **8.4a** (plain link submissions). **Consequences:** until 8.3, a pasted link points at the person's own file and uploaded submission originals stay in R2 with no retention cleanup (ADR-0010 holds; R2 free to 10 GB, then ~$0.015/GB-month; the Owner gets an R2 usage figure in 8A or the 6.6 checklist). Units regrouped: 8B = 8.4a, 9C removed, 10A = 10.2. PRODUCT §4.10/§4.13/§4.14/§4.15/§5 and ARCHITECTURE §11 carry the note.
- **Phase 3b review decisions (2026-09-28, owner; `/review-phase 3b`: architecture-reviewer + security review on `main...phase-3b`, exit-criteria audit):** must fix: (1) **a late End day has a cutoff**: yesterday's open day can be ended after midnight only until `org_settings.end_day_cutoff_time` (default **05:00 IST**, Settings → Thresholds, 00:00–11:59) and never once today's Start day exists; after it the day stays "End of day not recorded" and late work goes in an overtime note (migration `20260928063232_phase3b_review`, `attendance_end_day()` / `attendance_own_today()`, pgTAP `28`: 04:59 lands on yesterday, 05:01 refused with the message, refused after Start day; pgTAP `24` pins its late paths to a 23:59:59 cutoff). Owner answers folded in: (2) **comp leave is never on a day off** (`leave_submit_comp()` VALIDATION on a weekly off or a holiday; the leave form lists only working days, `comp_leave_dates()`); (3) **a holiday added or moved onto a comp leave date cancels that request with a reason and gives the credit back** (`holidays` trigger `app.holiday_release_comp()`, audited); (4) **a double tap on a standalone grant makes one credit** (the dialog's per-opening request key and in-flight guard; `comp_leave_grant(…, request_key)` returns the first credit, `CONFLICT` on another amount; e2e double tap). Should fix (owner: all): (5) comp leave without a credit through `attendance_submit` / `leave_request_change` added to the 3c follow-up migration list next to `leave_submit` (main's gate still offers it on staging); (6) pgTAP for `attendance_today_detail()` per role; (7) the Approvals group order (decision 29) in `e2e/expenses.spec.ts`; (8) the team month shows the unpaid **count** beside the total (decision 18); (9) `/reports/month` traces its rows (`loading-team-month`); (10) installed back specs for Settings → Expenses (a category's actions sheet and rename dialog), the "I'm working today" confirmation and the strip's day-off "I worked today" note. (11) **`expenses.spec.ts` and `month-summary.spec.ts` never ran at 430px** (missing from the `mobile-lg` project, so 3b.3's and 3b.4's installed back specs were checked at 375px only): added to `playwright.config.ts`. (12) **Found by the review's own e2e run: a refused Start day never refreshed**, so a Start-day prompt left open while the day started on another device (phone and laptop, both signed in) stayed up after "Your day has already started" until a reload (it surfaced as `edit-pattern.spec.ts` failing when two of its tests signed in as the same person at once). `StartDayButton` now refreshes after a refusal; `e2e/extra-work.spec.ts` "a prompt left open while the day starts elsewhere…" (red without the fix, green with it). **ROADMAP 3b.4 corrected:** Admins are rows in the month summary and the team report (employees); only the Owner views them; freelancers are excluded, which lands with the engagement field in 4.1. **Decision 28 confirmed as built:** the 3b app has no day-gate screen; the database keeps the gate's columns and functions for `main` until the 3c follow-up migration. **Accepted as built:** Admins and Staff claim (the Owner doesn't), a receipt only above ₹500, End day's expense question defaults to No, Mark paid on the person's Month tab, the summary counts decided days only, no one-tap approve for expenses. **Phone walk** (owner, Samsung S23, installed phase-3b preview, gesture navigation): every item on the 3b walk list passed. Decision 33 (the app guide's sections 2, 3, 5, 16, 17 to "Ready now") done in this review; the PDF is rebuilt locally (Edge on Windows).
- **Phase 3b added (2026-09-27, owner): the working day, comp leave, month summary, expense claims**, between phase 3 and phase 4, before the team starts using MaxOff. Its kickoff happened in conversation and is recorded below as "Kickoff 3b decisions", so it starts with `/run-phase 3b` on a `phase-3b` branch from `main` after the phase 3 merge. **Migrations in 3b must be expand-only while the phase is open** (the rule recorded after the staging-ahead deploy incident): 3b.1 reworks attendance, so old columns and functions are removed only after the merge. Rules changed in writing: CLAUDE.md invariant 2 (a member's own expense claims), ADR-0007 and ADR-0012 amendments, PRODUCT §4.2/§4.3/§4.3a/§4.11/§4.18/§5.
- **Kickoff 4 decisions (2026-09-28, owner; `/kickoff-phase 4` on branch `kickoff-4`, merged after the phase-3c review; recorded in PRODUCT §4.6/§4.13/§4.17, WORKFLOWS §1b/§3.1 "Settled at kickoff 4" and "Assignment warnings"/§3.3/§3.5/§9, PERMISSIONS ³/§3, DATA-MODEL §1/§2/§6, ROADMAP phase 4). `/run-phase 4` starts from `main` after that merge:**
  **4A (4.1, 4.2).**
  - (1) **The Owner is never an assignee** (`task_create` / `task_update_assignment` refuse it).
  - (2) **An Admin labels a task only with their own assigned clients**, so an Admin-created task always routes to the creating Admin; the Owner sets any label.
  - (3) When the Owner assigns **through an Admin**, any active Admin; a client label pre-selects that client's Admin.
  - (4) **`due_at` required**; a past deadline is refused at creation (later edits may move it anywhere); the dialog defaults the time to 6:00 PM IST and priority to Medium.
  - (5) **Bulk review is approve only** at both steps; a reject is one task with a reason.
  - (6) **Freelancers: no tasks-only login in phase 4** (revisit after the full launch; if ever offered: own tasks only, Task Noted, comments and Done, never attendance, leave, clients or People).
  - (7) **Freelancer → employee:** the Owner's "Invite as employee" attaches the login to the **same** `members.id`, sets engagement `permanent`, closes the coordinator row (`became_employee`); attendance starts the day after joining. Never employee → freelancer (deactivate and add a new record).
  - (8) **Coordinators:** any active permanent Admin or Staff, **many freelancers each, never the Owner**.
  - (9) **Freelancer payments later** (phase 9+): an Owner-only table read through `modules/revenue`, never on `members` or `tasks`; nothing in phase 4.
  - (10) **Done writes a `task_submissions` version** (built in 4A) with an **optional note that may contain http/https links**, tappable for the reviewer: the hand-in until phase 8; no uploads and no `submission_items` before 8.1.

  **4B (4.3, 4.4).**
  - (11) **Workload warning:** ≥ `workload_warning_threshold` (default **4**, Settings → Thresholds) open tasks due that IST day; freelancers count.
  - (12) **Overlap:** event windows overlap; no end = 1 hour; a date-only event never overlaps.
  - (13) **Leave warning:** approved leave, half day or comp leave on the due or event date, **plus pending requests** ("Leave requested"); never for freelancers.
  - (14) **No reminder editor in 4.3** (the type's defaults; the editor is 5.3's).
  - (15) **Task types are the Owner's** (guard on `settings.manage`, like expense categories); task custom fields stay `lists.manage`.

  **4C (4.5, 4.6).**
  - (16) **Notifications:** rows and recipients named in function comments, delivery with 5.1 (as 3b). Until then, the **Tasks tab badge** counts not noted plus changes requested (a coordinator's freelancers included), and the **Approvals badge** includes tasks.
  - (17) **Tasks tab first glance:**
    - Owner and Admins: **"Needs you"** first (overdue, not noted past the escalation time, waiting for your approval → Approvals), then open tasks by deadline, with the full filtered list one tap deeper.
    - Staff "My tasks": Not noted · Changes requested · Due today · Upcoming · Overdue, a coordinator's freelancers mixed in as "for Asha" (the order since (25): Overdue before Due today).
  - (18) **Freelancer reporting:** tasks completed, on-time %, acknowledgement lag, turnaround and rework count split by engagement in 6.3 and 9.3; the management list filters by engagement; the month summary already excludes freelancers.
  - (19) **Templates** shared company-wide; an Admin edits and archives their own (`task_templates.created_by`), the Owner any; tasks have **no stage presets** (typed on the task or from a template).

  **Added after the 4A review.**
  - (20) **Coordinator-change reason** (owner, 2026-09-29, after the 4A review): readable by the Owner and Admins only, like a deactivation reason; Staff coordinators, current or former, never read it.
  **Added after the 4B review** (owner, 2026-09-29, answering the review's S8 and S9):
  - (21) **Names on a task (ADR-0013 "every screen that names 'who' shows both"):** Staff see the names of **everyone who was on or acted on a task they can see**: its creator, current and past approvers, current and removed assignees, reviewers, whoever acted on it (comments, ticks, submissions, changes), and the current coordinator of a freelancer assignee. Names only (the phone rule of PERMISSIONS §2 is unchanged). Built in 4C's first commit: `app.directory_visible()` widened with pgTAP.
  - (22) **A task's client label is an Active or Paused client only** (a Draft client may have no Admin, and a labelled task routes to the client's Admin; WORKFLOWS §4 already refuses Inactive). The dialog offers Active and Paused; `task_create` / `task_update_assignment` refuse a Draft client when the label is set or changed (an existing label stays). Built in 4C's first commit, with pgTAP.

  **Added after the 4C review** (owner, 2026-09-30):
  - (23) **An Admin never decides their own suggestion.** An Admin's own suggestion goes to the Owner; the Admin can only withdraw it, never convert or decline it. Built in the 4C review fixes: `task_request_convert` and `task_request_decline` refuse (FORBIDDEN) when the caller is an Admin and `requested_by` is the caller (the Owner never suggests, so the Owner decides any); the Admin's "N suggested tasks to decide" count and its Needs-you row exclude their own (`countRequestsToDecide`); on `/tasks/requests` an Admin sees their own suggestion with Withdraw only (`requestActions`). PERMISSIONS §3, WORKFLOWS §3.4; pgTAP `38`, e2e `task-requests.spec`.
  - (24) **The requester is told when their suggestion is decided** (already an owner decision from the phase 5 kickoff, PR #21, "Kickoff 5 decisions" item 9): notified when their task request is **converted or declined**; a **withdrawn** request notifies nobody. WORKFLOWS §9 has the row ("Kickoff 5 decision 9, PR #21"); the `task_request_convert` / `_decline` / `_withdraw` comments name the recipients; delivery is phase 5's (no delivery code in phase 4).
  - (25) **Staff's "My tasks" order:** Not noted · Changes requested · **Overdue** · Due today · Upcoming (Overdue moved up from last). `MY_GROUPS` / `MY_LIST_GROUPS` in `modules/tasks/domain/lists.ts` (unit test), the list, the skeleton's comment, `task-lists.spec` asserts the order; PRODUCT §4.6.

  **Added at the owner's review of the task page (2026-09-30), built after the 4C review fixes:**
  - (26) **Task page first glance:** the title; one line with state, priority and a relative deadline ("Due today 6:00 PM · in 3 h", red when overdue); the "what's needed from you" line; and **only the next step as the primary action** (Task Noted → Start work → Mark done, one at a time; review actions for reviewers). Everything else (edit, reassign, cancel) under ⋯. On phones the primary action is a sticky bottom bar. Colour rule: one solid red commit per layer; no second solid button.
  - (27) **Four tabs below, a view control** (replace, never history, ARCHITECTURE §14.2): **Work** (stages/checklist and the Done hand-in note with its links; phase 8 uploads will live here), **Chat** (comments), **Activity** (history), **Details** (people with their Noted status, client, "Type: …", the approval route, created by, custom fields).
  - (28) **Chat:** the tab shows an unread count ("Chat · 2 new"). On phones Chat opens as a full-height bottom sheet (the thread, the composer at the bottom); back closes it. **Per-member read tracking:** a new table (expand-only migration, RLS for each role, pgTAP) storing each member's last read per task; opening Chat marks it read. Task rows in every task list show an unread-comments marker.
  - (29) **Activity:** the newest 5 with "Show all"; consecutive stage ticks/unticks by the same person within 10 minutes collapse into one line.
  - (30) **One display name everywhere:** the member's full name, not a username-style short name.
  - (31) **Every tab and the Chat sheet:** a tracing `loading.tsx`/skeleton, installed back-gesture specs at 375 and 430, and large-text checks.
  - (32) **Task page, phone vs desktop (addendum to (26)–(31)):** **Phone:** a compact header; the next-step action is a sticky bottom bar, hidden while the keyboard is open; the tab bar sticks under the header, with 44px targets, and scrolls horizontally at 200% text instead of squeezing; no horizontal swipe between tabs (it would fight the edge back gesture); Chat is a full-height sheet whose composer stays above the keyboard (`visualViewport`), and back closes it. A task opened from a list lands on **Work**, or on **the hand-in** when it is waiting for the viewer's review; tabs never switch by themselves. **Desktop (≥768px):** two columns: the main area with the header, the next step and the tabs Work · Chat · Activity; a sticky right panel with Details (no Details tab on desktop). Chat is inline as a tab.
  **Added at the phase 4 review** (owner, 2026-09-30):
  - (33) An approving Admin deactivated or made Staff: their `submitted` tasks go straight to the Owner and their open tasks lose that approver (route: Owner).
  - (34) A coordinator is treated like an assignee for the route: when the approving Admin coordinates a freelancer assignee, the task skips the Admin step and goes to the Owner.
  - (35) An Admin's suggestion without a client label is decided by the Owner only.
  - (36) Unread markers count only on tasks the viewer is on or decides (assignee, coordinator, approver or creator).
  - (37) Confirmed as built: a handed-in task opens on the hand-in for every viewer.
  - (38) Confirmed: read tracking (`task_reads`) is not audited; named as an exception in ARCHITECTURE's audit section next to `session_events` and `attendance_events`.

  **Carried constraint:** 3c decision 7, migrations stay expand-only across a release.
- **Kickoff 5 decisions (2026-09-29, owner; `/kickoff-phase 5` on branch `kickoff-5`, merges after PR #19; recorded in WORKFLOWS "Settled at kickoff 5" and §9a, PRODUCT §4.11/§4.16, PERMISSIONS §1 (`notifications.reachability`), DATA-MODEL §2 (`org_settings`) and §9, ROADMAP phase 5, ADR-0009 amendment). `/run-phase 5` starts from `main` after the phase 4 and phase 5 kickoff merges:**
  **5A (5.1, 5.2).**
  - (1) **Full-text push always**; title-only and per-device session tracking are dropped (ADR-0009 amendment). Subscriptions are removed only by "Sign out of this device" or deactivation.
  - (2) **Recipients:** never notify the actor; one row per person when they hold several roles. Added to §9: request converted or declined notifies the requester; a removed assignee is told; a new approving Admin gets the submitted notification; "Task Noted" notifies nobody; an Owner rejection notifies the assignees only.
  - (3) **From phase 6 on, every new transition function writes its rows through `app.notify()`**; 5.1 retrofits the existing functions (attendance, leave, comp leave, extra work, expenses, `client_assign_admin`, `end_day_reminder_due`, the 23:59 and 00:01 jobs, and phase 4's task functions).
  - (4) **In-app:** unread count on the bell, tap marks read and deep-links (parent list underneath), "Mark all read", opening a record marks its rows read, paginated history, **nothing purged** (revisit in phase 10).
  - (5) **Quiet hours** `quiet_hours_start/end` (22:00-07:00 IST, Owner-editable, no bypass except "Send a test"): push deliveries are `held` and go out at 07:00 as **one summary push per person** ("5 updates while you were away"; a single held row goes as itself); rows and email are not held.
  - (6) **Email fallback for no-push people: actionable kinds only** (assigned, changes requested, due-now and overdue reminders, escalations, leave/attendance/comp-leave/expense decisions); never for comments, "task changed" or info rows. The always-email set is unchanged.
  - (7) **Ceilings:** per person 20 and **org-wide `email_daily_cap_org` = 90** (Resend free = 100/day); over either = `skipped_cap`; invites, password and email-change mails are never counted or skipped; escalations and the digest bypass only the per-person cap.
  - (8) **Owner digest** `digest_daily`, worker job 08:00 IST daily, email only, no money, skipped when all counts are zero.
  - (9) **Banner per member:** everywhere, Owner included, no dismiss, permission on tap, denied shows how to re-enable; one working subscription on any device stops it on every device; other devices show a quiet "Turn them on here too" row on Me.
  - (10) **Owner setup, names only:** `RESEND_API_KEY`, `EMAIL_FROM` (`MaxOff <notifications@mail.maxoff.in>`, no reply-to), `VAPID_PUBLIC_KEY`, `VAPID_PRIVATE_KEY`, `VAPID_SUBJECT` (the owner's own `mailto:`), `CRON_SECRET`, in the GitHub environments. **The owner generates the VAPID keys on their own laptop when 5.2 is built; no session generates or prints them.** README's stale `mail.maxoff.app` was corrected to `mail.maxoff.in`. The 5.2 `/auth/confirm` prerequisite was done in 3cB review fixes (phase-3c branch), so it is not planned again.

  **5B (5.3, 5.4, 5.5).**
  - (11) **Default reminders:** 2 days before, 1 day before, "Due now" at the deadline, one overdue reminder 1 h after, plus the 24 h overdue escalation; an event also gets "tomorrow" at 18:00 IST with email; past-due fire times are skipped; nothing after Done.
  - (12) **Editor:** up to 5 "N minutes/hours/days before" rows; task's own, then type default, then org default, each **replacing** the one below; edited by whoever can edit the task; no mute; a moved deadline re-arms everything unsent, a reopen regenerates future ones; acknowledgement thresholds stay org-wide.
  - (13) **Leave, comp leave and holidays** pause acknowledgement repeats and before-due/due reminders for that person that day; assignment, change and decision notifications and every escalation still go out; weekly offs pause nothing.
  - (14) **Reachability:** joined Admins, Staff and the Owner only (not invited, not freelancers; a freelancer's coordinator is judged); the 48 h clock starts at first login or the state change; the Owner sees their own problem as a banner, never an alert; the Owner alert stays weekly per person; an Admin sees status and reason for members on open tasks they created or approve; the Owner also sees platform and last success; no endpoints; missing `RESEND_API_KEY`, `VAPID_*` or `CRON_SECRET` shows to the Owner as an operational warning.

  **Carried constraints:** 3c decision 7 (migrations expand-only across a release); ROADMAP 5.3's `logout_reminder` now reads `end_day_reminder` (built in 3b.1).

  **5A build decisions (2026-09-30, owner, before `/run-phase 5` unit 5A; binding, same weight as the kickoff):**
  - (15) **Task comment notifications:** every assignee, the creator, the current approving Admin and anyone who commented on the task before, **excluding the author**; in-app and push only, never email. Complements the task page's per-member comment read tracking (Kickoff 4 decisions 26-32).
  - (16) **Comp leave released by a new holiday** (`app.holiday_release_comp`): tell that person ("Your comp leave on <date> was cancelled because it's now a holiday; the credit is back").
  - (17) **A deactivated coordinator:** notify the Owner once per affected freelancer ("Asha has no coordinator: choose one").
  - (18) **Handing a client's work to another Admin** (`client_hand_over`): **one combined notification per new Admin**, listing the clients (not one per client).
  - (19) **"Take today as leave"** (`attendance_choose_leave_today`) notifies the Owner exactly like a normal leave request.
  - (20) **Task request routing:** a client label is Active or Paused only (kickoff 4 decision 22), so a Draft client cannot occur; if the labelled client has no Admin at that moment, the request goes to **the Owner only**.
  - (21) **The Owner digest (`digest_daily`, decision 8) moves to 5B**, after launch; 5A does not build it.
  - (22) **Workers plan is Paid** (active on the account); the stale "Free" comments in `wrangler.jsonc` are corrected in 5A. Cron triggers for `push_dispatch` go on staging and production.
  - (23) **Realtime runs in CI's e2e job** (`ci.yml` stops passing `-x realtime`), with an e2e check that Realtime works through the phase 4 hold proxy.
  - (24) **Money:** no amount in any notification text, title, body or payload. Comp-leave days (½ or 1) may appear in the person's **own** notification; extra-work minutes never appear.
  - (25) **People who lost access** (a removed assignee, a former coordinator, a client's previous Admin, a requester who is not on the converted task) get a notification that neither names nor links the client or the task they can no longer see; a converted request links to the request.
  - (26) **Environment names (the owner sets the values; no session generates, prints or asks for them):** GitHub environment **secrets** `RESEND_API_KEY`, `VAPID_PRIVATE_KEY`; **variables** `EMAIL_FROM`, `VAPID_PUBLIC_KEY`, `VAPID_SUBJECT`; all passed to the Worker at runtime through `deploy.yml`'s secrets file (nothing inlined at build time; the server hands the public key to the browser). `CRON_SECRET` is already set in both environments.
  - Build order: tables + `app.notify()` → retrofits → bell + history → push → email + quiet hours; each step pushed with a handoff. Model for today: Fable 5.1 (Opus 5.5 from 2026-10-01 07:30 IST).
- **Kickoff 3c decisions (owner, settled in conversation):** (1) **Staged launch, staff first:** production goes live with what is built (attendance, leave, comp leave, expense claims, the month summary, clients, people); each later phase is released to production as soon as it is reviewed, in the ROADMAP launch order; **no dates are planned**. (2) Staff tasks keep running outside MaxOff until phase 4 is released. (3) **No domain and no email service for stage 1:** production runs on its `workers.dev` address and people join through "Copy invite link" (sent by the Owner, for example on WhatsApp); the domain and Resend come before phase 5. (4) Production is a **separate Supabase project** (Mumbai region), never the shared staging database; staging stays for previews and testing. (5) **Backups before real data:** the nightly backup and one proven restore are part of going live, not after it. (6) The 3b follow-up migration (removing the 2.x day-gate columns and functions) runs in 3c, before production holds data. (7) From stage 1 on, **migrations stay expand-only across a release** and destructive changes ship in a later release. (8) **The Owner provides, before 3c can finish:** the production Supabase project id (keys go straight into GitHub, never through a session), the production R2 token and CORS and the `production` GitHub environment's secrets and variables (the same steps as staging), the staff list (name, email, role, job title), the month's holidays (for example whether Gandhi Jayanti is a day off) and the expense categories and receipt limit.
- **Kickoff 3c decisions, amended 2026-09-28 (owner, at the start of `/run-phase 3c`; these replace decision 3 where they differ):** (3a) **Address: production is `https://app.maxoff.in`, used everywhere** (`NEXT_PUBLIC_APP_URL`, Supabase Site URL, Redirect URL `https://app.maxoff.in/**`, R2 CORS, invite and recovery links, the guide, UptimeRobot). `maxoff.in` is Active on Cloudflare in the Workers' account; **`app.maxoff.in` is connected to the production Worker `maxoff` as a custom domain** (if the Cloudflare API token lacks the permission, the owner adds it in the dashboard: Workers → maxoff → Domains & Routes). **The bare `maxoff.in` and `www` are reserved for a future landing page:** no route, redirect or DNS record for them; Cloudflare's "visitors cannot reach maxoff.in" notices are intentional. **The `mail.maxoff.in` records belong to Resend:** never touched. **`maxoff.pixoraclips.workers.dev` redirects to `https://app.maxoff.in`** (path and query kept). (3b) **Email:** production Supabase Auth sends through custom SMTP (Resend on `mail.maxoff.in`, verified SPF/DKIM, DMARC `p=none`), sender `noreply@mail.maxoff.in` "MaxOff", 30/h; the Reset password template is the branded `supabase/templates/recovery.html`, subject "Set your MaxOff password". **"Forgot password works" is a step of the go-live smoke test.** The app's own email (`RESEND_API_KEY`) stays unset in stage 1: people still join through "Copy invite link". (3c) **Owner-done setup (verified by the owner):** project `maxoff-production` (Mumbai, ref `peshoflxypujbzecgwqq`) with the README "Hosted auth settings" (sign-ups off, password ≥ 12, OTP expiry 86400, secure password change); R2 token scoped to `maxoff-files-production`, CORS for `https://app.maxoff.in`; GitHub environment `production` with the variables and secrets the deploy workflow names (`SENTRY_AUTH_TOKEN` not set yet: source maps skipped); required reviewer the owner, no branch restriction (the workflow's own `v*` tag + `main` + green-CI guard applies). `SUPABASE_ACCESS_TOKEN` is fine-grained (Read everywhere, Write on Database and Migrations): a deploy failing on a Supabase permission is reported to the owner naming the permission. (3d) **Steps that stay the owner's:** approving the production deploy in GitHub; the Owner bootstrap, run in the owner's own PowerShell from the exact command the session gives (the secret key never passes through a session); UptimeRobot on the route the session names; entering the data with the session (staff list, this month's holidays, expense categories, receipt limit). **Staff invites go out only after the smoke test passes on `https://app.maxoff.in`.** (3e) **Owner note for 3c.3 (2026-09-28, given to `/run-phase 3c` at its resume, verbatim: "friendly placeholder copy before any invite"; the orchestrator's reading follows):** the stand-in screens a member can reach in stage 1 (`src/app/(app)/_placeholder/placeholder-page.tsx`: Tasks, an Admin's Approvals, Alerts, Calendar, an Admin's Reports, the Today / My Day tiles) say "<Screen> is filled in task N" with a build note; **before any staff invite goes out they carry plain, friendly copy in the app's voice** (what the screen will be for and that it is coming, no task numbers, no build words), for every role, checked at 375 and 430. (3f) **Owner note for 3c.3 (same message, verbatim: "go-live data is only the Owner bootstrap, seeded defaults kept"):** the only data entered into production at go-live is the Owner (`bootstrap_owner()` through the README's PowerShell); **the seeded defaults stay as the migrations seed them** (weekly off days, thresholds, expense categories and the receipt limit, job titles, lists); no staff list, holidays, categories or receipt limit are entered with a session (this replaces decision 8's data items and the "Owner's settings entered with him" part of ROADMAP 3c.3): the Owner changes them himself in Settings when the team starts. **Owner-side status (2026-09-28, from the owner's resume message):** GitHub environments `production` and `backup` complete; the Cloudflare deploy token has Zone permissions (Workers Routes, DNS, SSL) on `maxoff.in`; the age key pair exists (public key in `BACKUP_AGE_RECIPIENT`, private key offline); Resend SMTP and the Reset password template are live on production Supabase. Still the owner's, after the 3c merge: the first `v*` tag (**pushed from the laptop: tag pushes are refused from cloud sessions**, so `/run-phase` stops at the merge + tag step and gives the tag name and commit), the deploy approval, the bootstrap, UptimeRobot, the first backup run and the production restore drill.
- **Kickoff 3b decisions (2026-09-27, owner, settled in conversation; recorded in PRODUCT §4.2/§4.3/§4.11/§4.18/§5, ADR-0007 and ADR-0012 amendments, ROADMAP phase 3b; the units update WORKFLOWS / DATA-MODEL / PERMISSIONS as they build):**
  **Sessions and the working day.** (1) People **stay signed in**; the app never signs anyone out on its own. **"Sign out of this device"** lives under Me only (lost or shared device) and warns that notifications stop on that device; deactivation still ends every session at once. (2) **Signing in and out is no longer attendance.** `session_events` stay the security record; the working day is two explicit actions, **Start day** and **End day**. (3) **Start day replaces the blocking day gate.** On a working day the app opens freely; until the member has started or chosen leave, an in-app prompt says "Started working? Start your day to record it." with **Start day**, "On leave today? Choose leave" (Leave / Half day; **comp leave is not offered here**) and **Just looking**. It shows when the app opens or returns to the foreground, **at most once every 30 minutes**, until the day is started or leave chosen; My Day (Staff) and /today (Admin) carry a strip "Not started · Start day". **The start time is the tap, not the app opening.** There is no working-hours gate (hours are flexible). (4) **Days off** (weekly off, holidays): no prompt and no Start day; My Day offers **"I worked today"**, a day-off work note (below). (5) A working day with no Start day and no leave is "no submission": the 23:59 job proposes absent, unchanged. (6) **End day** records the end time and is **final for that day**: no resume. The app stays fully usable after it (checking tomorrow's tasks at 9 PM is not work). Its confirmation carries the optional overtime note and **"Any expenses to claim today?"** (below). Work after End day is recorded with an overtime note. (7) The 20:30 reminder becomes "You haven't ended your day" (started, not ended); the nightly flag becomes **end of day not recorded**; an End day after midnight lands on yesterday's day by the 2.1 late-logout rule. (8) The Owner stays exempt from all of it. (9) **Notifications show full detail on the lock screen for Admins and Staff** (they never hold money); **amounts never appear in push text, even the Owner's**.
  **Extra work and comp leave.** (10) **Overtime note:** the day (up to **7 days back**), rough duration (optional), what they worked on (required); from Attendance & leave or End day. (11) **Day-off work note** ("I worked today" on a day off, also up to 7 days back), note required. (12) Both land in the Owner's Approvals in a new **"Extra work"** group, after Leave: **Grant comp leave** (½ or 1 day) or **No comp leave** (reviewed). For a day-off note the Owner also decides whether to **mark the day as worked** (a worked-on-day-off day). Nothing is automatic. (13) The member sees the outcome on the note: "1 comp leave granted · use by 31 Oct" or "Reviewed by the Owner" (neutral, not "rejected"). (14) **The Owner can grant comp leave any time, independent of any note**, ½ or 1 day, with an optional note the member sees. (15) **A credit expires at the end of the IST calendar month it was granted in**, however close that is; expired credits stay in history as expired. (16) **Comp leave is used only through the leave form** (Request leave → Comp leave), offered only when a credit is available, showing the balance and the use-by date; it is removed from the Start day prompt. **The leave date must be on or before the credit's expiry** (the date counts, not when the Owner approves). **Using a credit is an ordinary leave request the Owner approves or rejects** (owner, 2026-09-27: a shoot or other work may make that day impossible), through the same pipeline as every leave; a credit only makes the option available. A submitted comp leave request **reserves** its credit so it cannot be requested twice; approval consumes it (oldest first); a reject, withdraw or cancel releases it unless it has expired. Comp leave is taken as a full day (1.0) or a half day (0.5) from the available total (owner-confirmed). (17) The Owner can **revoke an unused grant** with a reason the member sees.
  **Month summary (Owner only).** (18) Per person per IST month, **live at any time** and as a **month report for the whole team**: working days in the month, days worked, leave, half days (0.5 each), absent, comp leave used, **additional leave = full leave days + 0.5 × half days + absent days** (comp leave days never count), days off worked (its own line, not added to days worked), overtime notes (count and grants), comp credits granted / used / expired, and expense claims approved-and-unpaid (total and count). (19) **No monthly paid-leave allowance**, and **no salary stored or computed**: the Owner calculates pay. (20) Admins never see it; freelancers are excluded.
  **Expense claims (reimbursements).** (21) End day asks "Any expenses to claim today?" **No** ends the day; **Yes** asks for amount (₹), category, note and date, several per day; claims can also be added later from Attendance & leave. (22) Categories are an Owner-edited list (seed: Travel, Food, Materials, Other). (23) A receipt photo is optional, **required above an Owner-set amount (default ₹500)**; receipts are private files through `core/storage`, originals untouched. (24) Claim window: an expense dated in the current IST month, or in the previous month during the **first 5 days** of the new one. (25) States: submitted → approved → **paid** (Owner marks paid, optional date), submitted → rejected (reason shown), and the member may withdraw while submitted. (26) **Visibility:** the member sees their own claims, the Owner sees all, **Admins never see anyone's**, not even their team's (ADR-0007 amendment 2026-09-27). (27) Freelancers have no expense claims in the pilot.
  **Added 2026-09-28 (owner, before `/run-phase 3b`).** (28) **Shared staging while the phase is open:** every job and read that decides "attended" accepts **either** the 2.x gate choice **or** a Start day, so `main`'s app (still on the gate) and the 3b preview both work on the one staging database; `absent_check` must **never** propose absence for a day with a gate choice. The day-gate route, its cookie and the old columns and functions are removed only in a follow-up migration after the phase merges (expand-only). (29) **Approvals group order:** Attendance · Leave · Extra work · Expenses · Staff tasks · Client items. (30) The **team month report** lives at **More → Reports → "Month"** (Owner only; Reports' first real content); the per-person summary on `/people/[id]`. (31) The month summary reads days recorded before 3b (`first_login_at` / `last_logout_at`) as their start and end, so earlier months are complete. (32) **Reminders** (8:30 PM "You haven't ended your day", comp grant, expense decisions) are built as rows/recipients named in function comments; delivery arrives with 5.1, as in phase 2. **Nothing in 3b waits for notifications.** (33) At the end of `/review-phase 3b`, update `docs/guide/MaxOff-App-Guide.html` sections 2, 3, 5, 16 and 17 to "Ready now" with any detail that changed, rebuild the PDF with `docs/guide/build-pdf.ps1` and commit both.
- **3.3 storage mechanics (read before touching uploads, `/api/files`, the cron route, `worker/index.js`, `wrangler.jsonc` or the deploy workflow):** (1) **One S3 adapter** (`core/storage/adapter.ts`, `aws4fetch`, path-style URLs, retries nothing): MinIO in Docker for local dev and every e2e run (`pnpm storage:start` → `docker-compose.storage.yml`, bucket `maxoff`, console :9001, CORS for :3000 and :3100; CI's `e2e` job starts it; `playwright.config.ts` carries the throwaway `S3_*` values and `CRON_SECRET`), R2 on deployed staging and production through the **same five names** `S3_ENDPOINT` / `S3_BUCKET` / `S3_ACCESS_KEY_ID` / `S3_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY` / `S3_REGION` (+ optional `S3_PUBLIC_ENDPOINT` for a browser-facing host that differs). (2) **Upload protocol:** `beginUpload` (zod → permission: `logo` needs `settings.manage` or `clients.edit_assigned`, `avatar` anyone, `preview` names its original → `checkFile` limits → pending row → presigned PUT, or multipart above 8 MB) → the browser PUTs straight to the bucket (`client/upload.ts`, parts retried 3×) → `completeUpload` (HEAD must match the announced size; an SVG is read back, rewritten from the allow-list in `svg.ts` and PUT again; `file_complete()`); `failUpload` on a give-up. `uploadImageWithPreview` also makes a ≤ 512px JPEG on a canvas and uploads it as its own row (`preview_of`); when no preview exists `?variant=preview` falls back to the original (a raster as it is; an SVG as the sanitised, sandboxed document sent as an attachment, which an `<img>` renders and a direct visit downloads), so a tile never shows a broken image. (3) **Attaching is a plain column update** (`organizations.logo_file_id`, `members.avatar_file_id`, `client_brand.logo_file_id`): `app.files_reference_guard()` (not security definer, reads under RLS) requires a ready original image the caller uploaded (avatars refuse SVG), `app.files_archive_replaced()` archives the previous file and its previews; `storage_cleanup` deletes the object 30 days later. Status moves only through `file_complete` / `file_fail` / `file_mark_deleted` (`protect_columns`); a member may only UPDATE `name` on their own rows. (4) **`/api/files/<id>`** is public in the proxy and authenticates itself: the row is read under RLS (`app.file_visible()`), so an unauthenticated or unauthorised request is a **404**, never 403; `Cache-Control: private, max-age=86400, immutable` (a file never changes; replacing is a new id); every answer carries `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'none'; style-src 'unsafe-inline'; sandbox; frame-ancestors 'none'` (`FILE_ROUTE_CSP`, set by a later `headers()` rule in `next.config.ts`: a config rule replaces a header a route handler sets, which is why the route does not set it), an SVG is `Content-Disposition: attachment`, and a file is only ever an `<img>` source (`FileImage`, a plain `<img>` because the Next optimiser has no session). Downloads are 5-minute presigned GETs as attachments (`getDownloadUrl`). (5) **The cron:** `wrangler.jsonc` `main` is `worker/index.js`, which re-exports OpenNext's handler and the three Durable Object classes from `.open-next/worker.js` and adds `scheduled`; `triggers.crons` `30 21 * * *` (03:00 IST) at the top level **and** in `staging` and `production`; the handler POSTs `/api/cron/storage-cleanup` through `WORKER_SELF_REFERENCE` with `Authorization: Bearer <CRON_SECRET>` (`ctx.waitUntil`). The route compares the secret timing-safely, is POST-only (a job that deletes is never reachable by a safe method), refuses everything when `CRON_SECRET` is unset (reported to Sentry once outside `local`), runs `runStorageCleanup` with the service client (`file_cleanup_candidates` batches of 200: archived ≥ 30 days, `pending` or `failed` ≥ 24 h; delete object → `file_mark_deleted`, a failure on one object never stops the rest) and answers a JSON report. The Preview workflow's `wrangler versions upload` bundles `worker/index.js` on every push, which is the proof it builds. (6) **deploy.yml is NOT yet updated for storage (stop list, production workflow):** the secrets file needs `S3_ENDPOINT`, `S3_BUCKET`, `S3_ACCESS_KEY_ID`, `S3_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY`, `S3_REGION`, `CRON_SECRET` from the environment's `R2_*` values and `CRON_SECRET` (the exact `jq` lines are in the 3A report / README "Storage"); until then a deployed staging has no bucket and uploads fail with "File storage is not configured", and the cron route answers 401. **Staging R2 exists (owner, 2026-09-27):** bucket `maxoff-files-staging` (APAC, private), a token scoped to it, CORS origins `https://maxoff-staging.pixoraclips.workers.dev` + `https://*-maxoff-staging.pixoraclips.workers.dev`, methods GET/PUT/HEAD, headers `content-type`, `content-md5`, `x-amz-*`, `ExposeHeaders: ETag`; GitHub `staging` environment: secrets `R2_ACCESS_KEY_ID`, `R2_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY`, `CRON_SECRET`, variables `R2_ACCOUNT_ID`, `R2_BUCKET`. Production bucket exists; token/CORS/secrets wait for the production domain. (7) **Screens 3A added or changed, for the phase-3 phone walk:** Settings → Company (logo card + upload sheet), `/me` (photo button + upload sheet, avatar in the header), the user menu avatar, Settings → Custom fields (`/settings/custom-fields`: tabs, add/edit sheet, archive/restore). (8) The e2e spec `storage.spec.ts` skips the logo test on the phone projects (one organization row; the desktop project owns it); the photo test uses `avatar-<project>@` people and clears the photo first, so it re-runs without a reset.
- **Freelancers (owner decision 2026-09-27, ADR-0013, docs only; built in phase 4):** a freelancer is a `members` row with `engagement = freelance`, no auth user, no invite, exactly one **coordinator** (active permanent Admin or Staff, history in `member_coordinators`) who acts on their behalf from their own account with actor = coordinator + `on_behalf_of` = freelancer in the task rows and `activity_log`; notifications route to the coordinator; attendance, leave, the gate and the jobs are `permanent`-only in SQL; roles unchanged. The rejected alternative (the coordinator using the freelancer's login) and why are in the ADR. PRODUCT §3/§4.13/§4.16/§4.17/§5, DATA-MODEL §0/§1/§6/§9, PERMISSIONS §1/§3, WORKFLOWS §1b/§3.1/§9 and ROADMAP 4.1–4.5 carry it; the open questions are listed under phase 4's units for `/kickoff-phase 4`.
- **Deploys carry their secrets in the version (PR #10, 2026-09-27):** the first `main` deploy after phase 2 failed with Cloudflare error 10215 (`wrangler secret bulk` is refused while the Worker's newest version is an undeployed branch preview, which every push has left since task 2.0). Both deploy jobs now write `.wrangler-secrets.json` (required `SUPABASE_SECRET_KEY`, the two optional secrets when set; gitignored, removed in an `always()` step) and run `wrangler deploy --env <name> --secrets-file`, one deployment for code and secrets (wrangler 4.135, additive). **Never reintroduce `wrangler secret put` / the action's `secrets:` input before a deploy.** A new Worker secret joins the `jq` object in `deploy.yml`, README → Deploying and `.env.example`.
- **The suites run on a working day, whatever the calendar says (2026-09-27, PR #9, merged to `main` before phase 3):** the first IST Sunday since phase 2's tests exist went red on `main` and `phase-3` (pgTAP `07` test 59 saw `is_day_off = true`; the Owner's board spec found nobody expected) because the seed's weekly day off is Sunday. Now pgTAP `07` pins the weekly day off to tomorrow's weekday and clears a holiday on today (as `11` and `13` did), and the **e2e global setup does the same through PostgREST with the service key** (`e2e/calendar.ts`: `pinWorkingDay`, `pinnedOffDays`, `describeOffDays`, unit-tested in `tests/e2e-calendar.test.ts`), logging "today is a weekly day off (Sunday); running with Monday off instead"; `settings.spec` asserts the pinned days. **A new pgTAP file or spec that assumes a working day gets it for free; one that needs a day off sets it itself and restores the pin.** Proof on the Sunday: pgTAP 1040/1040, full e2e 432 passed / 81 skipped, `pnpm check` green. Found on the way, and the reason a targeted local run still failed one test: the local pg_cron jobs fire at 23:59/00:00 IST on a running stack and left **209 system-proposed `pending_review` days**, more than the Approvals list's `PENDING_LIMIT` (200, oldest first), so today's real rows fell off `/approvals`; a full run's `owner-bulk` sweeps them (2.5 note 11), a targeted run after an overnight stack needs `pnpm db:reset` first.
- **The build process changed on 2026-09-26 (owner decision, not a roadmap task; shipped with phase 2):** work is done in **units** (ROADMAP `Units:` lines from phase 3; tier = the highest of the unit's tasks); a phase starts with **`/kickoff-phase N`** (every open business question at once, answers recorded in the docs and as a "Kickoff N decisions" block here), is built by **`/run-phase N`** (one general-purpose subagent per unit, [H] → fable / [C] → opus / [Q] → sonnet, the orchestrator verifies each unit, a handoff at every boundary, stop conditions in CLAUDE.md "How to work") or unit by unit with `/start-task <unit>` (plan, then proceed when the kickoff settled the questions); the real-phone walk is one per phase in `/review-phase`; **`preview.yml` now applies a `phase-*` branch's migrations to staging on every push when staging is missing any** (`supabase migration list --linked`, then `db push` only for pending ones; the manual dispatch and the refuse-on-main guard stay; the preview build waits for it and its PR comment says whether staging has the schema). Phase 3 therefore begins with `/kickoff-phase 3`.
- **Phase 2 review (2026-09-26, Fable 5.1; architecture-reviewer + security review of `main...phase-2`): no must-fix.** Verified: RLS on the three new tables with no API write path at all (no write policies, privileges revoked, `protect_columns`), per-role pgTAP allowed and denied; every transition function `security definer` + `set search_path = ''` with permission → scope → state → write → audit; Admins `FORBIDDEN` from every decision; the `leave:` lock order consistent across all 11 functions and both jobs; jobs on `app.job_day()`, idempotent; no `new Date()` / `now()::date` outside `core/time`; money untouched; `maxoff_home` only ever sends a member to a whitelisted home matching their own JWT `sub`; the day pass is HMAC'd; the early Sentry queue inherits the SDK's scrubber. **The [H] check of 2.9 passed** (`EditableRecord` meets the DoD, the back order edit layer → Save confirmation → Discard dialog adds no spare entry, installed specs at 375/430). **Fixed before the merge, one commit each:** (1) `/api/approvals/approve` threw on `Origin: null` or garbage (500, not 403): `core/http/origin.ts` `sameOrigin()` + unit test; (2) `EditMemberDialog`'s `pending` only wrapped `setError`, so Escape/backdrop could unmount it mid-save and a failure was silent: a saving flag covers the round trip; (3) the Log out overtime note looked up today's day only, so after midnight it was refused while the logout landed on yesterday: `attendance_flag_overtime_today(reason)` (migration `20260926174147`) picks the day by the logout's rule, pgTAP 15; (4) a leave request had no span cap (`9999-12-31` accepted): `app.leave_validate()` refuses more than **365 days** for every writer (migration `20260926174801`, owner decision: a 26-week maternity leave must pass), mirrored in zod (`LEAVE_MAX_DAYS`), unit + pgTAP + a `leave.spec` refusal; (5) correcting an approved-leave day to Present/Absent left the leave approved silently and `worked_on_leave` false: **owner decision 2026-09-26** — Present on a date covered by the linked approved leave sets `worked_on_leave` (like an approved "I'm working today"), the leave is never touched by any correction, the dialog says so for every status but the leave's own type (migration `20260926175013`, WORKFLOWS §1, pgTAP 15). Local proof before the fixes: 432 e2e passed / 81 skipped after one `db:reset`, 1018 pgTAP, 763 unit; after: pgTAP 1040, `leave.spec` 16/16 (full runs recorded in the session log). **Device check (step 3, owner, 2026-09-26): Samsung Galaxy S23, installed app, gesture navigation, light and dark mode — all seven items pass** (gate never returns on back; Leave tabs, month pager, request sheet and confirms; Approvals sheet → dialog → back order, Approve all, Undo adds no entry; Today → person → tabs/months → one back to Today, ⋯ on a People card; /me Edit → "Discard changes?" on back, Log out warning; launch intro, slide in/out, no zoom, largest font; status bar readable in both themes after a reinstall).
- **2.8 mechanics and numbers (read before touching imports of client components, `core/observability`, the pre-hydration script, `bundle-budget.json` or the Worker build):** (2026-09-25, `4d5d87d`, `c8b5b1e`). **Done:** (a) browser Sentry deferred behind an early reporter (`core/observability/early.ts`, ARCHITECTURE §18.2); client components out of every barrel, per-file imports from `app/` (ADR-0011 amendment, lint + `tests/module-components.test.ts`), zod-free `domain/limits.ts`, `core/errors` split for the browser, `core/time/clock.ts`; (b) `pnpm budget` (`scripts/bundle-budget.mjs`, `bundle-budget.json`) in `pnpm check` and CI, failing when a route is over or nothing is budgeted; **taps before hydration** (§14.2 l): `PRE_HYDRATION_SCRIPT`, a per-link `data-live` hand-over (`markLive` callback refs on `BackLink`, `ViewLink`, the bar's tabs), one case table for app and script, `e2e/pre-hydration.spec.ts` (375, 430; a control run with the script disabled failed 3 of 3). **Found by CI on `4d5d87d`:** a first version marked the whole document hydrated from the root layout; the root hydrates before the streamed shell, so the e2e `hydrated()` helper (switched to that mark) let three specs act before the shell listened, and a back control in a still-streaming page would have fallen through as a push. Fixed with the per-link mark; `hydrated()` waits for `data-chrome` again. **Numbers** (before `091815c` → after `c8b5b1e`; probes `scripts/bundle-budget.mjs --all`, `scripts/measure-hydration.mjs`, `scripts/measure-coldstart.mjs`): first-load JS `/login` 686.6 → **558.8 KB** (gzip 216.6 → 177.5), `/today` and `/my-day` 1440.7 (identical to `/approvals`) → **745.8 KB** (gzip 419.0 → 238.4); hydration (`html[data-chrome]`, 4× CPU, 375 px, local `next start`, median of 9, two rounds) `/today` Owner 985 / 1022 → **937 / 937 ms** (blocking 301–338 → 271–275), `/my-day` Staff 890 / 902 → **814 / 805 ms** (blocking 286–287 → 186–205); Worker upload 33,816 → **31,514 KiB** (gzip 7,321 → 6,838); cold start (fresh preview version, signed-out `/login`, 50 requests on fresh connections, split at 1 s) 6 cold, median 2305 (1629–2823) → 6 cold, median **2221 ms** (1475–2940); warm median 322 → 310 ms. **Why hydration barely moved although the JS halved:** a trace at 4× shows the window is now framework-bound: evaluating the react-dom chunk (~170–270 ms), React's hydration pass (~260 ms), microtasks (~180–280 ms) and first style and layout (~175 ms, not forced). `/my-day` has only 185 elements and still takes ~800 ms. The document itself is streamed in ~50 ms locally. So the fix for the 2.7b symptom is the pre-hydration script (the ~0.8–1 s window now behaves correctly, only without the slide), not a faster hydration. **Cold start findings (part c), local workerd in a Linux container** (OpenNext cannot build on Windows: symlinked `node_modules`; built in Docker with `node:24`): the first request after a restart costs ~0.87–0.91 s vs ~35 ms warm. Of that, **~0.65 s is route-independent** (a cold `/robots.txt`) and ~0.28 s is `/login`'s own chunks. Sentry's server DSN on/off: no difference (init is not the cost). **Removing Sentry from `instrumentation.ts`**: route-independent cold **0.65 → 0.47 s**, Worker 31.5 → 27.2 MB; `/login` still loads it through `core/errors/action.ts`. The Worker bundle carries the Node SDK + OpenTelemetry (~3.6 MB) twice before 2.8 (RSC and SSR layers; the SSR copy is gone now), and zod twice. On staging a cold isolate appears whenever a fresh connection lands on one (5 minutes idle did not evict), so the probe splits requests at 1 s. **Owner decision 2026-09-25:** yes to `@sentry/cloudflare`, as its own task **6.5b [H]** (ADR, scrubbing parity test, staging before/after with the same probe; under ~250 ms gain → record and don't switch). Without it, what is left is Next/OpenNext's own bootstrap (~0.47 s locally). **Worker builds run in Docker** (OpenNext cannot build on Windows: symlinked `node_modules`): `git ls-files -co --exclude-standard` → tar → `node:24` container, `pnpm install`, `pnpm build:worker`, `wrangler dev --env staging` for local cold starts (kill `wrangler-dist`, `esbuild --service` and `workerd` between rounds, or they leak and the VM runs out of memory). Keep the `node:24` image until 6.5b is done (owner). **Faster hydration exposes "hydrated ≠ content ready" in specs** (CI #74 on `f9abe47`, 2026-09-25, `mobile.spec.ts` "the brand bar hides on a flick"): the shell (`data-chrome`) now hydrates before a route's streamed content is revealed, and the screencast showed the flick landing on the `/people` skeleton, which cannot scroll. Root cause fixed in the spec's precondition (wait for the list's first card), no timeout or retry. A spec that needs page content must wait for that content, not for `hydrated()`.
- **2.7b mechanics (read before touching navigation links, `BackLink`, the `(app)` layout, the tab bar, row layouts or the head scripts):** (1) **The slide needs a typed navigation:** a new drill-down link is `DrillLink` (or `OverlayLink` from inside an overlay); a plain `Link` to a detail works but does not slide. `RouteTransition` is keyed by the pathname (a template would not remount inside a segment), so a path change remounts the page **and every nested layout under `(app)`** (keep no client state in a nested layout that must survive a drill-down); a query-only change remounts nothing. **Tab routes of one screen share a key** (`routeKey`): `/leave` + `/leave/attendance` and `/people/[id]` + `/attendance`, whose header and tabs now live in `people/[id]/layout.tsx` (moved out of the two pages in 2.7b; the name streams in behind its title skeleton, the views' `loading.tsx` trace only the view, `loadPerson` is `cache`d). Before that, every tab switch rebuilt the header and tabs and flashed their skeleton (measured: 3 skeleton pieces in the header band on `/people/[id]`, and the `/leave` layout remounted under the path key). A new screen with tab routes adds a `routeKey` entry and a layout. A second tap on back during a slide is ignored (in-flight guard). (2) **`BackLink`'s back is a hand-started view transition** (`slideBack` in `core/ui/motion/slide.ts`): React renders the transition Next starts in `popstate` synchronously, in the microtask checkpoint right after Next's listener, so a type can be added neither before (no transition to join yet) nor after (already committed); both were tried and proved by the spy. The swap waits for the `popstate` plus one task, capped at 500 ms. (3) **Per-tab scroll is `tab-history`'s** (`tab-scroll.ts`): saved on every tab move, restored on arrival and held for 1 s against Next's scroll-to-top and streaming, released by any touch, wheel or key, and forgotten once it has run, so a remounted bar never re-applies it; module state, so a reload starts at the top. (4) **`STANDALONE_SCRIPT` runs on every document** (unlike the launch intro), before it in `<head>`; it relies on Next writing the viewport meta at the top of `<head>` (checked in a build: it does), and appends a locked copy. `"standalone" in navigator` is the iOS check. (5) **Large text:** card and list rows use `CARD_ROW_TITLE` (`flex-[1_1_6rem]`) and `CARD_ROW_TRAILING` (`ml-auto`) on a `flex-wrap` row, so at the default size nothing changes and under large text the trailing part drops under the title; a new row layout should use them. The Approvals group wraps too: "Approve all" drops under the heading, and a name too long to share its line puts the status underneath. `mobile.spec.ts` checks the main screens at 130% and 200% (no sideways scroll, nothing past the edge, an ellipsis keeps 64 px); a new screen joins `LARGE_TEXT_SCREENS`. (6) **Refresh on return** (`refresh-on-return.ts`): the wall clock through `systemClock()` (not `performance.now()`, which stops while a phone sleeps, so an overnight return would read as seconds; `Date.now()` is refused by lint, ADR-0008), throttle 30 s from mount, skipped while `anySendWaiting()`. It re-runs the `(app)` layout, so the day gate applies after midnight IST exactly as on any navigation. e2e fakes `visibilityState` and fast-forwards Playwright's clock.
- **2.7 mechanics and launch numbers (read before touching `/`, the proxy, the icons or the root layout):** (1) **`/` is answered by the proxy** (`core/auth/session.ts` → `rootRedirect()` in `core/auth/home-hint.ts`): signed out → `/login`; the httpOnly cookie `maxoff_home` = `<user id>|<home>` (Next URL-encodes it on the wire; `request.cookies` decodes) matching the JWT's `sub` and naming a real home → one 307 to it; anything else falls through to `src/app/page.tsx` (reads the member). Never `/` → `/`. Set by `login` and `setPassword` (both now redirect to `homeFor(role)` directly) and refreshed by `submitDayChoice` and `issueDayPass` (the daily pass), so a role change is stale for a day at most; the Owner refreshes at sign-in only (role cannot change). Owner decision 2026-09-25: a cookie, not an access-token hook (blast radius). (2) **One geometry for the launch** in `core/ui/pwa/launch-screens.json` (`background`, `markSize` 144, `maskableMarkFraction` 0.6, the 11 iPhone screens with their status-bar heights): `scripts/generate-icons.mjs` renders the maskable icon and `public/icons/startup/*.png` from it, the root layout lists the screens as `appleWebApp.startupImage`, and `launch-intro.tsx` draws the mark at `markSize`; `pwa-files.test.ts` holds them together (safe-zone arithmetic, PNG IHDR sizes, the intro's path = `icon.svg`'s). Re-run the generator after changing either file. The 144 px / centred position is derived (Android 12+ draws the adaptive icon at 240 dp, 60% of it = 144 dp; iOS from our own image) and **needs the device recording to confirm on the S23**; if the mark jumps at the hand-off, adjust `markSize` (and a vertical offset if needed) and regenerate. (3) **The intro is CSS only:** `LAUNCH_INTRO_SCRIPT` in `<head>` sets `html[data-launch]` for an installed window's first document (`sessionStorage` key `maxoff-launched`), the cover fades in 300 ms with `forwards`, so it stays in the DOM hidden (`visibility: hidden`) — tests check hidden, not absent. (4) **Touch CSS** is in a new base block at the end of `globals.css`. (5) **Launch numbers** (`node scripts/measure-launch.mjs`, local `next start`, fresh context signed in, 375 px, median of 9, two rounds each; "before" = `--no-hint` on the same build, the pre-2.7 path): the `/` redirect **18–24 ms → 5–6 ms**, first paint **136–156 ms → 108–152 ms**, the screen's header visible **144–222 ms → 105–205 ms** (Owner, Admin, Staff; the header number includes Playwright's polling). Signed-out `/`: **8–12 ms → 3–4 ms** locally (curl). **Staging before** (curl, signed out, `/` rendered `page.tsx`): **2.70 s cold, then 0.14–0.55 s** (the roadmap's 829 ms was a signed-in sample); **Staging after** (owner, 2026-09-25, `phase-2` preview, Owner, median of 5 each, in run order; ms): 1 hint: redirect 1036, ttfb 2444, fcp 2780 (first run, cold) · 2 no-hint: 378, 546, 648 · 3 no-hint: 1183, 1873, 2008 (first run after a pause, cold) · 4 hint: 658, 1458, 1852 (still warming) · 5 no-hint: 406, 560, 724 · 6 hint: 268, 534, 792. **Owner's conclusion: no regression.** The slow rows are Worker cold starts on either path; warm, the hint saves ~110–140 ms on the redirect, and paint is within noise. The cold start itself (1.3–2 s for the first request after idle, on either path) is not the redirect's; it went to the 2.8 line (owner decision 2026-09-25). **2.8 numbers re-measured** (same probe on HEAD and on this build, decompressed): `/login` JS 13 files **693 KB → 693 KB**, HTML 21.6 → 27.8 KB; `/today` JS 23 files **1450 KB → 1450 KB**, HTML 146 → 152 KB. No client JS added (the intro is a server component plus a ~400-byte inline script); the HTML grew by the 11 `apple-touch-startup-image` links, which Next also repeats in the RSC payload. (6) **Not done here, on purpose:** §14.2 j and g (2.7b); sheet swipe-down, a pending state on ordinary `Link`s and the measured 100 ms pressed state (Ideas).
- **2.5 mechanics (read before touching the jobs, `attendance_touch()` or `attendance_logout()`):** (1) **The job date is derived, never read off the clock:** `app.job_day(now(), '23:59')` is the most recent IST date whose 23:59 has passed (unit-tested at the boundaries in pgTAP `13`), so a late pg_cron start still processes the day that ended and an early one repeats yesterday as a no-op. **Every run covers the last 7 IST dates, oldest first** (owner decision 2026-09-25): a missed night (outage, paused free-plan project) is filled on the next run because both jobs are idempotent (a day in any state other than "no row" / `awaiting_choice` is left alone; a flagged day is not flagged twice). A single date can be passed for a manual re-run as `postgres` (`select * from app.absent_check('2026-09-24')`); a date whose 23:59 has not passed is `INVALID_STATE`. (2) **Two pg_cron rows, one minute apart:** `absent_check` at `29 18 * * *` (23:59 IST) and `logout_not_recorded` at `30 18 * * *` (00:00 IST); `cron.schedule(name, …)` upserts by name, `cron.job_run_details` is the run record. **Locally the jobs really fire at 18:29/18:30 UTC on a running stack**, inside the known no-e2e window around midnight IST (2.6 note 8), so a dev database left running gains proposed absences for the seeded people every night; `pnpm db:reset` clears them. (3) **Nothing on a day off**, leave-derived days included, and a row already marked `is_day_off` is never proposed; `app.is_working_day()` answering null (no single organization in scope) makes the job **fail** rather than act. (4) **The Owner is exempt by data:** the job takes active members whose role holds `attendance.self` (`role_permissions`), attendance started (`app.to_ist_date(joined_at) < date`), in id order, each under the `leave:` lock before any row lock (pgTAP `14`, dblink, like `12`: fixtures ids `0e…21/22` sort before the seed so the job reaches them first; **dblink tests only run through `pnpm db:test` (TCP), not through `docker exec psql` on the socket**). (5) **Proposed absence keeps `first_login_at`** when the row was `awaiting_choice` (the review sheet shows "Signed in HH:MM"); a no-row person gets "Signed in: Not recorded". One `proposed_absent` event and one audit row either way, actor null. (6) **`app.attendance_open_day(member, date, first_login)`** holds the leave-or-awaiting derivation; `attendance_touch()` calls it with `now()` under `touch:` → `leave:`, then stamps `first_login_at` on any row that lacks it (audit `first_login`), which also covers the race the 2.4 comment described (the job opening today's row a moment before the touch: cannot happen before 23:59, but the path is the same). (7) **A logout after midnight clears `logout_not_recorded`** (owner decision 2026-09-25): `attendance_logout()` sets `last_logout_at` and `logout_not_recorded = false` in one update; the audit diff holds only the columns that changed, so 07's "holds last_logout_at alone" still passes for a normal logout and `13` asserts the two-column diff for the late one. (8) **The Owner's summary notification and the 20:30 reminder are 5.1's** (no `notifications` table yet): `app.absent_check()` returns `(work_date, member_id, day_id, outcome)` for every day it wrote, the list 5.1 will notify from, and the recipient is named in the function comment as every 2.1 function does. (9) **The proposed-absence e2e arranges the row through `serviceInsert`** (`attendance_days` + `attendance_events`, which `service_role` may write); running the real job in e2e would propose absences for every seeded person and pollute Approvals for the parallel specs. The job's own proof is pgTAP. (10) `00_core_base` counts the internal `app.*` helpers (now 12): add every new service-role-only helper to `app_internal` there. (11) **Proof that the suite survives an overnight stack (owner request, 2026-09-25):** one `db:reset`, a full `pnpm test:e2e` (288 passed / 62 skipped, 4.5 min), then both jobs run as `service_role` for the last 7 dates (`set role service_role; select * from app.absent_check(); select * from app.logout_not_recorded();`: 207 proposed absences over 30 seeded people, 0 logout flags because the e2e sessions' days are today's, which the jobs never touch before 23:59), then the full suite again on that database: **288 passed / 62 skipped, 3.5 min, nothing changed.** Why it holds: every spec that owns people deletes their days at its start (`resetAttendanceAndLeave()`, 180 of the 207 rows went that way), and the 27 proposed days of people no spec owns were swept by `owner-bulk`'s "Approve all" (it approves whatever is on screen, and its assertions are on its own two people, which were within `PENDING_LIMIT`). The Approvals badge and count assertions are "at least" checks. **So the cron rows stay active locally and 13 asserts them as scheduled**; if a future spec asserts an exact Approvals count or a person's history first row on a past date, it is that spec's fixture that has to reset the person, never the job.
- **2.6 root causes (2026-09-25), one per fix, each reproduced before it was touched.** Rule kept throughout: no timeout raised, no retry added; the three 15 s allowances (2.2, 2.3) were **removed**. (1) **Logout back-stack flake (`back-gesture.spec.ts:259`, `auth.spec.ts:75`) = Next 16 wiping the overlay marker.** Reproduced 8 of 23 with `--repeat-each=10` on both phone projects (four "stayed on /my-day with the confirmation pending", four "back from /login landed on /my-day"), 10 of 10 with tracing on. The trace showed the logout request leaving **5 ms** after the confirm tap, i.e. `closeOverlaysThen` never backed out; a live probe (`.impeccable/probe/marker-probe.mjs`) showed `history.state.maxoffOverlay` present at 915 ms and gone at 1083 ms, right after the day-pass action's response, while a dialog opened 2.5 s later kept it. Cause: Next's `HistoryUpdater` runs `replaceState` with a fresh state object after every navigation, refresh or completed server action, and `segment-cache/navigation.js` sets `preserveCustomHistoryState: false` on every replace navigation, so a server action finishing while an overlay is open strips the marker from the overlay's own entry; `<IssueDayPass />` answers a second after landing on My Day, exactly when the test taps Log out. Fix (`core/ui/overlay/overlay-history.ts`): `replaceState` is wrapped once (`guardMarker` / `keepMarker`, unit-tested, and the Next contract is pinned in `overlay-history.test.ts`); `closeOverlaysThen` backs out **only when the controller owns the top entry** (`pushedCount > 0` and the marker) and waits for the popstate with **no timer** (owner rule: no fallback may fire while a back is in flight); the 300 ms fallback is gone. `auth.spec.ts:83` also clicked an unscoped "Log out"; it is scoped to the dialog. (2) **"Cold start" = first-render storm, not a slow action.** Measured on an idle machine (`.impeccable/probe/coldstart.mjs`): first `GET /login` 505 ms then 46 ms, first sign-in 543 ms then 120 ms, so a route's first render costs ~0.5 s. In the suite the moment `setup` finished, three projects' workers hit every screen and the bottom bar prefetched five routes per page: the first test's trace shows RSC requests of 1.5–3.8 s and a logout action over 5 s. Fix: `e2e/warm.setup.ts` (last in the serial `setup` project) requests every route once as the Owner before any parallel project starts; the allowances in `signIn()`, `passGate()`, `chooseAttendance()`, `auth.spec.ts:12/:68` and `back-gesture.spec.ts:279` are back to Playwright's defaults. (3) **GoTrue after `db:reset`:** the reset restarts the auth and realtime containers and the database (containers 25 min old vs 39 h for the rest); probing right after a reset, GoTrue's health and password grants answered 200 within a second and PostgREST answered too, so the 2026-09-24 "three setup sign-ins INTERNAL" run **could not be reproduced**; the only mechanism that fits is PostgREST still reconnecting to the restarted database (a 503 on `session_login` maps to INTERNAL). `e2e/global-setup.ts` now waits for GoTrue, for PostgREST to answer an rpc (proof it reached Postgres) and for Mailpit before the build; a readiness check with a two-minute ceiling and a named reason, not a timeout. (4) **`shell.spec.ts:78` (Staff sent to No access) never failed in this session's runs** (it passed the baseline in a telling 14 s, inside the first-render storm). Ruled out by reading: setup rewriting the staff state (dependents start after `setup` ends), a refresh race (tokens live 1 h, a run is 3 min), another spec ending the shared session (only `leaver@`/`reset@` are logged out or changed). The one code path that sends a signed-in person to `/login` with nothing wrong with their session was `updateSession()` and `getSessionState()` treating **any** `getClaims()` error as "no session": closed by (5). If it ever fails again, the retained trace will show the landing URL; that is the next datum. (5) **Transient auth failure ≠ signed out** (owner decision): `core/auth/session-errors.ts` `classifySessionError()` (transient: `AuthRetryableFetchError`, 5xx, 429, non-auth exceptions; invalid: no session, refused refresh, bad JWT — an expired session never reaches the error page), the proxy passes a transient failure through, `getSessionState()` throws `SessionUnavailableError` (digest `SESSION_UNAVAILABLE_DIGEST`), both error boundaries render "Can't reach the server. You're still signed in." + Try again through `describeBoundaryError()` (`core/errors/boundary.ts`), Sentry still gets the error. Unit tests both ways. (6) **Offline page (`production.spec.ts:118`)** passed every run here; the assumption it made silently — that the reloaded page is *controlled* by the worker, which `serviceWorker.ready` does not promise — is now waited for as state (`navigator.serviceWorker.controller !== null`), which is also what its recorded failure ("Sign in · MaxOff" from the network) means. (7) **Mailpit:** `latestEmailTo(to, since)` takes a message newer than a timestamp taken before the request (2 s slack for the Docker VM clock), `clearMailbox()` is deleted, and the recovery flow puts `reset@`'s seeded password back in `afterAll`. Mailpit keeps messages across `db:reset` (a message to `gone@` from an earlier day was still there). (8) **Midnight IST:** the baseline full run crossed 18:30 UTC and 36 Admin/Staff tests failed at once, every one landing on `/attendance?next=…` (the gate for the new day). Not a code flake, but a cascade nobody should have to read: `global-setup.ts` records the IST date and `global-teardown.ts` names a run that crossed midnight. **Known limit (owner decision 2026-09-25): a run that crosses 00:00 IST fails broadly because of the day gate; CI must not be scheduled across midnight IST (18:30 UTC), and a local proof run is not started within ~15 minutes of it.** (9) **Five runs after one reset** needed `team.spec.ts` (invitee@, moved@, pending@, typo@, fixed@ removed in the Owner block's `beforeAll` through `removeFixturePerson()`, sign-in included) and `settings.spec.ts` (`removeJobTitles()` at the start of the two job-title tests) to clean their own fixtures. (10) **Local workers are capped at 4** (`playwright.config.ts`, owner decision 2026-09-25): Playwright's default of half the cores put eight Chromiums beside the 7.5 GB Docker VM on this 16 GB laptop; the first proof run had one sign-in stall at 6.4 s (GoTrue's grant 3353 ms against a 216 ms mean over 101 grants, Postgres under 60 ms: the whole machine paused), and the second was killed by the harness for critically low host memory during `next build`. A resource setting, not an allowance: **a sign-in stall at four workers is a real cause to investigate, never "machine load".** (11) **Not changed on purpose:** the local `sign_in_sign_ups = 30` per 5 min rate limit was never hit (no 429 in any run); `retries` is 0 everywhere and `trace: retain-on-failure` (owner decision). (12) **Found by the proof itself, `mobile.spec.ts:140` on /people:** a strict locator on `[data-slot="page-header"]` threw at once because two headers existed for a few milliseconds — the route's `loading.tsx` draws the same header (§14.1) and React streams the resolved page into the DOM **hidden** (`<div hidden id="S:1">`, seen in the trace's DOM snapshot) before its `$RC` script swaps it in; a strict-mode violation is not retried. `pageHeader(page)` in `e2e/helpers.ts` selects the **visible** one; every spec goes through it. (13) **Also found by the proof (run 2 on the used database):** `day-gate.spec.ts` assumed its `gate-*` people had no day yet, so every gate test failed on the second run (`resetAttendanceAndLeaveOf(email)` at each test's start now); and the job-title test's archived-row assertion and its "Restore" click were ambiguous once the Admin test's archived "Sound Engineer" sat beside "Colourist" (the Restore button carries no per-item name) — the test scopes to its own row and both tests remove all three names first. The first `removeJobTitles()` also filtered on `list_key=job_titles`; the key is `job_title`, and a DELETE that matches nothing is still a 204, so a cleanup helper must be checked against the rows once. **Proof (2026-09-25, 4 workers, browsers closed, one `db:reset` at 00:24 UTC, then five `pnpm test:e2e` in a row): 285 passed / 0 failed × 5, 207 s · 211 s · 219 s · 207 s · 203 s; across the five runs GoTrue answered 540 password grants at a 102 ms mean, 326 ms max, none over a second.** Note for reading the numbers: 285 is the count with `mobile.spec` and the other desktop-only or phone-only blocks skipped where they do not apply. The e2e wall-clock reads live in `e2e/run-state.ts` `wallClock()` behind one documented lint exception; the ADR-0008 rule stays on for specs.
- **2.4 mechanics (read before touching Approvals, the Owner's Today or `/people/[id]`):** (1) **Undo is a delayed send** (`core/ui/delayed-sends.ts`, WORKFLOWS §1 "Settled in 2.4"): `ApprovalGroup` holds each approval 6 s and sends at once on `visibilitychange` hidden, `pagehide` or unmount; the send is a `keepalive` POST to `/api/approvals/approve` (`postKeepalive`; the route runs the same server action, same-origin only), because a server action's request dies with the page. Sending dismisses its Undo toast; an Undo tapped too late says "Already sent". A failed send, the network included, puts the row back with its message. Nothing is recorded unless kept, so there is no un-approve function. (2) **Approve all** sends the ids on screen through one action that loops `eachId()` (`core/errors/bulk.ts`: sequential, per-row results); the data layer retries a row once on `40P01` (`core/db/retry.ts`). (3) A dialog opened from a review sheet is held **next to** the sheet (`ReviewSheet` stays open): back closes dialog, then sheet, then screen. A link inside an overlay is `OverlayLink` (backs the overlay's entry out first, then pushes); DataTable's detail-sheet `actions` close the sheet by state, so a link there belongs in `detail` (People's "Attendance & leave"). (4) `PageHeader back` renders `BackLink` everywhere (Settings sections too): Navigation API `currentEntry.index > 0` goes back, otherwise replace with the parent. **In Playwright the first page sometimes has `about:blank` beneath it and sometimes not**, so a back-stack check after a direct open asserts "not the page", never "stays". (5) The badge is computed in `(app)/layout.tsx` (`navBadges`, two head counts, Owner only) and passed as `AppShell badges`; every decision revalidates `"/", "layout"`. (6) `attendance_touch()` takes the `leave:` lock too when it opens today's day (touch: → leave: → rows; the review found a first sign-in could derive a day from leave the Owner was cancelling). `ReasonDialog.onSubmit` may resolve `false` to stay open with the typed reason. `attendance_today()`'s generated types say non-null; `getTodayPeople()` reads the rows loosely. (7) e2e people: `review-{day,fix,leave}-<project>@` (`review-local-password`) and `review-bulk-{a,b}@`; `serviceInsert()` arranges states no flow reaches (a clash). `--repeat-each` on `owner-review` races its own `beforeAll` (same people); repeat single tests with `-g`.
- **Back-stack fix, §14.2 a, c and e (fix(ui), 2026-09-24):** (e) the sign-in, set-password and logout server actions `redirect(…, RedirectType.replace)` (a server action's redirect pushes by default; the gate's render-time redirect and the choice action already replaced, `/auth/confirm` answers with an HTTP redirect). **Logout from the confirmation first backs out the dialog's own history entry** (`closeOverlaysThen`), because the replace would otherwise replace the dialog's entry and leave the page under /login: any future server-action redirect started from inside an overlay needs the same. (a) `DropdownMenu` and `Select` roots register with `useOverlayOpenState` like `Sheet`/`Dialog` (`overlay-registration.test.ts` checks all five).
