(c) More destinations and Me are tab roots: `BottomNav`'s `topLevel` includes them, `useTabNavigation` exposes `handles(href)`, and a More link (installed only) calls `closeOverlaysThen(() => navigate(href))`: back out the sheet's entry, then push from home or replace from another tab. `closeOverlaysThen` falls back after **300 ms** if the popstate never comes, runs **at most once**, and ignores a second call while one is under way; the More sheet also takes one destination per opening. A page that is **not** a root (e.g. `/settings/job-titles`) still pushes normally from More. The desktop sidebar keeps normal web history (owner decision). Specs: `e2e/back-gesture.spec.ts` (installed, 375 and 430): sign-in, gate, settled sign-in, logout and recovery each leave nothing underneath (seeded `back-mobile@` / `back-mobile-lg@`, password put back through the GoTrue admin API: `recoveryLinkFor()`, `setPasswordFor()` in `e2e/helpers.ts`, local stack only); select → dialog → page; menu → page; More → People / Me from home and from another tab; a double tap lands once. **Data for 2.6:** a run started straight after `pnpm db:reset` failed all three `setup` sign-ins with INTERNAL (GoTrue still restarting); the same run a minute later was green.
- **My Day polish (2026-09-24, after 2.3):** the attendance card is gone. **`TodayAttendanceStrip`** is one line on My Day and the Admin's /today (`describeTodayStrip()`: "status · standing" from `STATUS_LABELS` / `CHOICE_COPY`, dot by state; a tap opens `/leave/attendance`, or the gate while no choice is made); on an approved-leave day it keeps "I'm working today" (PRODUCT §4.2's banner) as a small trailing button. **Overtime** moved out: `OvertimeLogoutNote` inside the Log out confirmation (injected by the `(app)` layout through `LogoutProvider extra`, because core never imports modules; it takes part through `useBeforeLogout()`, and `ConfirmDialog.onConfirm` returning `false` keeps the dialog open for its field message), and "Flag overtime" on today's entry in `/leave/attendance` (`canFlagOvertime()`). **Log out** is `LogoutRow` at the bottom (PlaceholderPage `footer`). **/leave is two routes now:** `/leave` (requests) and `/leave/attendance` (the month), under `leave/layout.tsx` (header + `LeaveTabs`, awaits nothing, so it stays painted), so each view's `loading.tsx` traces it; the requests pager shows only past one page. `loading-routes.test.ts` accepts a header drawn by the route's own layout. **Gotcha met:** an action inside the DataTable detail sheet that opens a dialog must keep that dialog **above** the sheet (the sheet unmounts when an action is chosen): `OvertimeDialog` is held by `AttendanceHistory`, as `LeaveRequestList` holds its dialogs. The phone e2e caught it.
- **Back-gesture fix (fix(ui), 2026-09-24, after 2.3):** (1) **Detail-sheet bug: not reproducible after 1.5, device-checked on S23** (Samsung Galaxy S23, Android, installed app, gesture navigation). The sheet was already registered (`DataTable` renders our `Sheet`); only regression cover was added: ESLint `no-restricted-imports` refuses `radix-ui` / `@radix-ui/*` outside `core/ui/primitives` (merged into the DB-client and domain blocks through `restrictedImports()`, because flat config replaces a rule's options per block; fixtures in `tests/lint-fixtures`), and installed-mode specs in `e2e/back-gesture.spec.ts` (/people direct and through More, the sheet → confirm hand-off, /leave's day sheet), which now also runs in `mobile-lg` (430px). **Playwright cannot show a real browser's back-gesture rules on pushed entries**, so a real-phone check stays on the phase-review list. (2) **New bug, fixed:** /leave's tabs and pager were plain `<Link>`s, so each switch pushed history (three tab switches took three backs to leave; found on the S23). **Rule (ARCHITECTURE §14.1): in-page view controls never add history, on any device.** `ViewLink` (`core/ui/composites/view-link.tsx`) is a `Link` with `replace` plus an immediate pending state (`useLinkStatus`); tabs pass `scroll={false}`. `view-links.test.ts` sweeps the source (query-building `<Link>`, tab arrays feeding `<Link>`, `router.push` with a query, client `redirect()` with a query) with inline failing fixtures; a query link that goes to another page is marked `view-link: navigation`. The audit found no other page with URL-synced controls. (3) **The seed now dates every member's `joined_at` 40 days back** (was 30), so the attendance history always has a previous month to page to.
- **2.3 mechanics (read before touching `/leave` or the leave module):** (1) `leaveRequestActions(request, today)` in `modules/leave/domain/requests.ts` is a **contract with the SQL**: its unit test writes the rules out independently (state × source × open change × timing: future, ongoing, ends today, ended), and pgTAP `10` holds the database side of the same matrix. **Fully past approved leave is closed to the member** (the row says "This leave has ended. Ask the Owner to correct it."). Change a member-facing leave function and both must change together. (2) "Has an open change" is read with a second small query (`supersedes_id` of the member's `submitted` rows), because the change can sit on another page. (3) The change row's original is a PostgREST self-embed `original:leave_requests!supersedes_id(...)`; the generated types call it to-many, the repository accepts either shape. (4) The system's own reasons are read as data and reworded: `leave approved` / `leave cancelled` on attendance events, `cancellation approved` on a cancelled original. **If a migration changes those literals, `domain/history.ts` and `leaveDecisionNote()` must follow.** (5) `e2e/helpers.ts` `resetAttendanceAndLeave()` / `serviceSelect()` use `SUPABASE_SECRET_KEY` and **refuse any host but 127.0.0.1/localhost**. A spec that owns fixture people should reset them the same way instead of relying on `db:reset`. (6) The e2e people are `leave-self-{desktop,mobile,mobile-lg}@maxoff.local` (`leave-local-password`; the mobile-lg one is an **Admin**). (7) On a phone, Escape closes a detail sheet **through history** (overlay-history), so a test that taps another card straight after must wait for the sheet to be hidden.
- **Staging migrations (checked 2026-09-25):** `pnpm supabase migration list` works from this machine now (the CLI is linked) and showed every migration through 2.4 applied on staging; **2.5's `20260925010221_attendance_jobs.sql` is on staging too** (owner, 2026-09-25: "Remote database is up to date"). Future ones: push with `pnpm supabase db push` (it pushes every pending one in order), or through the Preview workflow's `staging-migrations` once `preview.yml` is on `main`.
- **2.2 rule A (owner decision 2026-09-24):** a day the Owner has decided (approved **or** corrected) stays when a later leave is approved over it, and comes back in `kept_dates`; "the leave wins" only over a day still waiting (`pending_review`), an unanswered day and a derived day. 07's expectation for an approved Present was changed accordingly (it was "corrected to the half day").
- **e2e cold start (data for 2.6):** on 2026-09-24 one full run took 3.7 min instead of ~1.8, and every early desktop sign-in took 5-6 s; `auth.spec.ts` "an unsafe next value is ignored" used the raw form with the default 5 s expect and failed once. It now has the same 15 s allowance as `signIn()`. The next run was 194/194 in 1.8 min.
- **2.2 day gate mechanics (read before touching `(app)` layout, `core/auth/gate.ts` or the attendance card):** the layout and its page render **in parallel**, so a page never assumes the layout's `attendance_touch()` has run: `touchToday()` is `cache()`d per request and the attendance card awaits it when it finds no row; **Next memoizes identical GET fetches for a whole request**, so a re-read after a write in the same render must opt out (`getOwnDay(..., { fresh: true })` passes an abort signal). The pass cookie is set by `<IssueDayPass />` (server action, asks the database again) or by the choice action, never by the layout. The seed now dates every member's `joined_at` 30 days back (attendance starts the day after joining) and adds 12 `gate-<kind>-<project>@maxoff.local` people for `e2e/day-gate.spec.ts`, named "Test …" so People's first 10 phone cards are unchanged. `e2e/helpers.ts` `signIn()` answers the gate with Present by default (`{ gate: "stop" }` to meet it); `passGate()` waits for the gate's options **or** a shell title bar, never the URL. pgTAP 01-06 now delete attendance/leave rows first, so `pnpm check` still passes after a Playwright run. **`DAY_GATE_COOKIE_SECRET` is set in the `staging` GitHub environment (Owner, 2026-09-24); `production` needs it too before launch**; without it every page load calls `attendance_touch()` and Sentry gets one warning per Worker instance.
- **Two known e2e flakes are both ROADMAP 2.6, and neither is "just re-run it".** The Mailpit recovery race, and `e2e/shell.spec.ts:78` (Staff sent to No access), which failed once in a full parallel run on 2026-09-23 and has passed in isolation and in every full run since. Confirmed **not** a regression from the 1.5 diff. 2.6 carries the symptom, what has already been ruled out (`team.spec.ts` deactivates a dedicated `USERS.leaver`, not the shared `staff` account) and what to check next. **If either fails for you, that is data for 2.6 — capture the run, do not just re-run.**
- **2.1 decisions (2026-09-23, owner-approved), in DATA-MODEL §3 and WORKFLOWS §1/§2:** reasons are **optional** for every member submission and **required** only for the Owner's reject / correct (`REASON_REQUIRED`); the Owner may correct a day in **any** state (an `awaiting_choice` correction ends the gate for that day); `attendance_decide()` decides a linked `submitted` request with the day, and `leave_withdraw()` / `leave_decide()` refuse `source = attendance` (**the attendance day is the single door**); a new request overlapping the member's **`submitted` or `approved`** request is `CONFLICT`, and rejected / withdrawn / superseded / cancelled rows **never block** (tested explicitly); an employee's cancellation is a `submitted` row with `requests_cancellation = true` whose approval ends **both** rows `cancelled`, so "approved leave covering a date" is always `state = approved`; a cancelled or superseded leave **returns today's untouched derived day to `awaiting_choice`** (system correction, reason "leave cancelled"), past days stay; a logout after midnight lands on yesterday's day and writes `last_logout_at` + the `logout` event **only** (pgTAP asserts the audit diff holds that one column); two approved requests covering one date → the most recently decided derives the day. **Mechanics worth knowing:** `attendance_touch()` is `returns table`, so its output column names are plpgsql variables and `on conflict` must name the constraint (`attendance_days_member_date_key`), and PostgREST hands it back as a one-row array; `attendance_events.id` is a **bigint identity** because one transaction shares one `now()` (order history by `id`, never by `at`); the audit trigger consumes `app.audit_override` on the first audited write, so every function sets it **right before each** `attendance_days` / `leave_requests` write (the events table is not audited and does not consume it); `attendance_touch()` also writes `session_events(login)` when the member has none on today's IST date, matched with `app.ist_day_start()` so the `(member_id, at)` index applies; `is_day_off = (app.is_working_day(today) is false)`, so the null answer is "not a day off". **For 2.5:** the 23:59 job creates `pending_review` days with `final_status = absent`, `proposed_by_system = true` and an `attendance_events` row `proposed_absent` through `app.attendance_event()`, and approved leave for anyone who never logged in through the same derivation `attendance_touch()` uses (factor it out of touch into `app.attendance_open_day()` then); `attendance_decide('approve')` already handles the proposed absence (tested). Nothing sets `logout_not_recorded` yet (2.5).
- **`pnpm test:e2e` needs a clean database. Run `pnpm db:reset` first.** On a database left over from `pnpm db:test` or an earlier e2e run, `settings.spec.ts:132` (job title add/rename/reorder/archive) and `team.spec.ts:38` (invite link) fail on the `desktop` project — the seeded job title and invitee already exist. Confirmed 2026-09-23 by stashing and running them against the committed tree: **they fail there too, so it is never a regression in your diff.** After `db:reset` the suite is 163 passed / 0 failed across the four projects. This costs a real debugging detour if you meet it cold.
- **Branch previews are live (2.0). Push, then open the phone.** `.github/workflows/preview.yml` builds every non-`main` push and uploads it as a *version* of the staging Worker. Three URLs, all in the job summary and in one PR comment that is edited in place: **`latest-maxoff-staging.pixoraclips.workers.dev`** (rolling, follows whichever branch pushed last — the permanent bookmark), `<branch>-maxoff-staging.…` (precise), and `<version-prefix>-…` (pinned to one commit). The branch alias is the branch name lowercased with non-`a-z0-9-` turned into dashes, `b-`-prefixed if it starts with a digit, hash-truncated past the 63-char DNS label.
  - **A preview never deploys and never migrates.** `versions upload` writes no triggers and promotes nothing; `wrangler versions deploy` appears nowhere in the repo. So **a branch that adds a migration previews against a staging database without it** and the feature will error — that is expected. To apply them: Actions → **Preview** → **Run workflow** → the branch → `staging-migrations`. It refuses `main` and shares the `deploy-staging` concurrency group so it cannot race a `main` deploy. Migrations pushed that way stay on staging until the branch merges.
  - Previews build with `NEXT_PUBLIC_APP_ENV=staging`, so they get the staging security headers and `robots.txt: Disallow: /` for free. **Measured 2026-09-23: Cloudflare's preview edge replaces `X-Robots-Tag` with its own `noindex` on every preview response** (staging serves the app's `noindex, nofollow`), so a preview is noindexed regardless of the build and only `nofollow` is lost — immaterial for a sign-in-gated app — **if a later task adds an environment-dependent header, previews inherit whatever `staging` gets.** They reuse the `staging` GitHub environment's variables and secrets, so **the `staging` environment must keep "deployment branches: All branches" and no required reviewer**, or every preview fails before its first step. The Worker secrets are inherited from the live staging deployment (wrangler uploads with `keepSecrets: true`); the workflow deliberately never runs `wrangler secret put`, because that publishes a deployment.
  - Two versions per push (one per alias) is expected in `wrangler versions list`, not a bug: one version can hold only one alias.
  - **The `staging-migrations` Run workflow button only appears once `preview.yml` is on `main`** — GitHub lists `workflow_dispatch` from the default branch only. Until phase 2 merges, push a branch's migrations with `pnpm supabase db push` locally.
- **1.5 decisions (2026-09-23, owner-approved), in ARCHITECTURE §14.1:** **mobile is its own layout for every role.** The bottom bar replaces the hamburger drawer for Owner and Admin too (`src/core/ui/shell/mobile-nav-sheet.tsx` is deleted, and `e2e/production.spec.ts`-style assertions in `e2e/mobile.spec.ts` keep the bar in place). The split lives in **`MOBILE_PRIMARY` + `MOBILE_MORE` in `core/ui/shell/nav.ts`** and nowhere else, in the owner's stated priority order — today, approvals, tasks, calendar, reports, clients, people, settings: Owner and Admin both get **Today · Approvals · Tasks · Calendar · More**, Staff keep PRODUCT §4.7. Clients is deliberately out of the bar (set up once, visited occasionally). **Owner and Admin are separate entries although they match today**, because Approvals and Reports will weigh differently once they carry numbers. `nav.test.ts` proves the two arrays together are exactly the role's navigation. **Revisit the split after the pilot from what people actually open.**
  - **`NavItem.badge`** renders as a count on the bar icon, the More row (More shows the sum hidden behind it, `totalBadge()`) and, since 2.4, the sidebar. **2.4 sets it on Approvals** through `withBadges()` from the layout; **5.1 adds Alerts** (unread notifications) the same way.
  - **The brand bar hides on scroll down and returns on scroll up** (`chrome-scroll.ts`, a pure reducer with its own unit tests: 12px threshold, always visible in the top 48px, iOS rubber-band negatives ignored; `mobile-chrome.tsx` sets `data-chrome` on `<html>` and CSS does the rest). **If it feels wrong on a real phone, the agreed fallback is a bar that simply scrolls away and only returns at the top of the page**: delete `MobileChrome` from `AppShell`, drop the `html[data-chrome="hidden"]` rules in `globals.css`, and make `[data-slot="top-bar"]` `static` below `md`. That is a known switch, not a rewrite.
  - **2.2 must also put Log out on My Day for Staff.** End-of-day logout is a first-class action (it records the time, WORKFLOWS §1), not a menu item; today Staff reach it through the Me tab.
  - **Log out asks first, everywhere** (`core/auth/components/logout-confirm.tsx`). The confirmation is a provider **above** `AppShell`, because the account menu and the More sheet both close when you choose an item and a dialog rendered inside either would unmount with it. All three entry points call `useRequestLogout()`.
  - **The mobile rules are base CSS, not per-screen classes** (`globals.css`): 44px targets, 16px inputs, `touch-action: manipulation`, overscroll containment, safe-area variables. A screen built in a later phase inherits them. **A button is matched by `[data-variant][data-size]`, never `[data-slot="button"]`** — Radix `asChild` lets a trigger overwrite `data-slot`, so the account menu's button reports `dropdown-menu-trigger`; keying on `data-slot` silently missed every trigger in the app (the 44px sweep in `e2e/mobile.spec.ts` caught it).
  - **`PageHeader` is the mobile title bar**: sticky, 44px, with the back chevron, the title and (for Owner and Admin, who have no Alerts tab) the bell. `description` is desktop-only; a phone gets the optional `help` sheet instead. It absorbed `SettingsBackLink`, which is deleted. Its background is **opaque on purpose**: `backdrop-filter` makes an element a containing block, which positioned the `fixed` primary-action FAB against the bar instead of the viewport and pushed it off screen.
  - **`DataTable` takes a `mobile` card spec** (title / subtitle / trailing / detail / detailTitle / actions) and renders cards + a detail sheet below `md`, 10 at a time with "Show more". Table and cards are swapped by CSS, not by a media-query hook, so nothing flashes on load and the hidden one is out of the accessibility tree. `Dialog` **and** `AlertDialog` become bottom sheets below `md` from one shared `modal-surface.ts`; the footer stacks with the primary action **above** Cancel so the thumb rests on the harmless button.
  - **`devIndicators: false` in `next.config.ts`.** `{ position }` still works in Next 16, but with a bottom bar on every role there is no free corner: it sat on the More tab. Next still surfaces every compile and runtime error without it.
- **Staging checks from phase 1 are both DONE (2026-09-23).** The proxy behaves on the Worker (`/today` → 307 `/login?next=…`, `/login` 200), and the **owner verified deactivation on staging by hand**: a test member signed in in a second browser, was deactivated from People, and their next click landed on `/login?reason=inactive`. Also: staging now runs on **Workers Paid** — the free plan's 10 ms CPU limit returned Error 1102 on `/set-password` (ADR-0003, amended).
- **Supabase stays on the FREE plan (owner decision 2026-09-23, ADR-0003 amended).** Consequences already written into the docs: leaked-password protection is Pro-only so it is **off** (invite-only + 12-character minimum cover it); **Supabase keeps no backups**, so the nightly `pg_dump` to R2 in task 6.6 is the only safety net and is not optional; ~1 day of database log retention; 500 MB database and 5 GB transfer per month. **Watch the dashboard Usage page monthly**; upgrade at ~4 GB transfer or ~400 MB database. Until then: select only the columns a screen needs, paginate every list, and plan to archive `activity_log` rows older than a year.
- **1.1 decisions (2026-09-22), all in DATA-MODEL §0a/§1/§9, PERMISSIONS §2/§3, ARCHITECTURE §5:** identity helpers live in the `app` schema (`app.current_org_id()`, `app.current_member()`, `app.has_permission()`); policies call them as `(select app.has_permission('x'))` so they run once per statement. **`app.in_transition()` is owner-based** (true when `current_user` is not `authenticated`/`anon`), so a transition function needs no flag: being `security definer` is enough, and pgTAP tests of the "owner path" just run as postgres. `app.protect_columns('col', ...)` guards `members(status, invited_at, joined_at, deactivated_at, email)`; every later state column gets the same trigger. `app.audit_row_change()` skips no-op updates and `updated_at`; pass the id column as its argument when the pk is not `id` (`org_settings` uses `org_id`). Admins read other people only through **`member_directory`** (no email; phone included); the base `members` table shows a full row only to its owner and to `team.manage`. A member edits only their own `full_name`/`phone` (`app.members_self_edit_guard()`); the Owner row can never lose its role outside a transition. `org_settings` is created by trigger with the PRODUCT §7 defaults. `activity_log` and `session_events` have no INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE grant for `authenticated`: login/logout go through `session_login()` / `session_logout()` (1.2), never a client insert. Deferred columns: `members.job_title_id` (1.3, with `list_items`), `members.avatar_file_id` and `organizations.logo_file_id` (3.3). **Staff do not hold `notifications.reachability`** (PERMISSIONS §1 says "own devices only", a scope note the drift test refused to read as a grant): 5.4/5.5 show a member their own devices through RLS on `push_subscriptions`, not through the key. **`tests/permissions-drift.test.ts`** compares PERMISSIONS.md §1, `ROLE_GRANTS` and the migration seed; a later migration that changes `role_permissions` must be added to its `MIGRATIONS` list. The local seed holds the organization and, since 1.2, five sign-ins (README → "Local sign-ins").
- **The auth seam (1.2, ADR-0012, ARCHITECTURE §7a):** `getSessionState()` in `src/core/auth/server.ts` (per request, `cache()`) → `none | inactive | member`; `getCurrentMember()` is the member-or-null view of it; **`requireMember()`** is what layouts and pages call (signed out → `/login`, inactive session → `/auth/signout` → `/login?reason=inactive`). `requirePermission(key | keys)` builds on `requireMember()` and redirects to `/forbidden` without the key. **Actions use `assertPermission()`** (throws `AppError`, so `action()` returns a `Result`). `CurrentMember` has `id`, `email`, `role`, `name`, `jobTitle` (null until 1.3). `src/proxy.ts` only refreshes cookies and redirects from the JWT (`core/auth/session.ts`); its public-path list is `core/auth/paths.ts`, and **`/api/*` is public there**, so every future route handler authenticates itself (cron secret, webhook signature). The sign-in forms live in `core/auth/components` and the app layout passes `<LogoutMenuItem />` into the shell, so `core/ui` never imports `core/auth`. **`members` has column-level UPDATE grants** (`full_name, phone, role` for `authenticated`): 1.3 adds `job_title_id` and 3.3 `avatar_file_id` to that grant in their migrations, and every later table with state columns follows the same pattern (revoke update, grant the editable columns, `protect_columns` trigger). `service_role` is "in transition" (pgTAP documents it): jobs call transition functions for state columns. 1.2 must also drop the `core/auth/server.ts` → `core/ui/shell/preview-viewer` import when it deletes the shim, so `core/ui` never stays an auth dependency. Review of the 1.1 diff (architecture-reviewer) found and fixed: TRUNCATE/REFERENCES/TRIGGER were still granted to `authenticated` (now revoked on every public table, tested), a member could be inserted already `active` (now `app.members_insert_guard()`), `members_update_manage` WITH CHECK lacked the permission test, and the activity-log policy's "own actions" clause would have leaked audit diffs of Owner-only tables to the Admin whose action triggered them (dropped; scope is per entity only).
- **Staging is live (first deploy 2026-09-22, Deploy run 35706683648):** `https://maxoff-staging.pixoraclips.workers.dev`, Worker `maxoff-staging`, Supabase project `jcejebyjaqwcumyzrhge` (Mumbai) with the base migration pushed. Verified by curl: `/` 200, `/today` `/dev/ui` and every shell route 404 with no preview member, `/offline` 200, `sw.js` with `Cache-Control: no-cache`, all six security headers present, source maps uploaded to Sentry. **`@sentry/nextjs` runs on the Worker but its default `node:https` transport never completes there** (proved 2026-09-22 with a staging-only `debug: true`: "Flushing events..." then "Done flushing events" exactly at the 2 s timeout, 0 events at Sentry); `core/observability/transport.ts` (platform `fetch`) replaces it on the server and edge runtimes. The `@sentry/cloudflare` worker-wrapper fallback was not needed. The real blocker was the Node SDK's default `ContextLines`, `Modules`, `LocalVariablesAsync` and `Context` integrations (filesystem / inspector; they never answer on Workers, so processing stalled to the 2 s flush timeout while client reports still arrived); `server.ts` drops them and the flush now takes ~400 ms. **Verified end to end on 2026-09-22 (issue MAXOFF-1):** the event arrived with every fake value `[scrubbed]` in title, context (incl. `billing`), extra and tag; no request URL, headers, cookies or body; no user id or IP. The diagnostic also caught a scrubber gap: `contexts.nextjs.request_path` carried the query string (only Sentry's server-side password rule hid it); `scrubValue` now reduces `url` / `href` / `from` / `to` / `request_path` keys anywhere to origin + path. To troubleshoot again, set `debug: true` in `server.ts` on a branch, deploy to staging, read Workers Logs, then remove it: the SDK logger prints the **unscrubbed** event message. `wrangler secret bulk` created the Worker itself on the first run, so the secret-before-deploy ordering is fine. Still to do by hand: install the PWA on desktop and phone from the staging URL. **`GET /diagnostics/sentry`** (staging builds only) throws a deliberate error with fake money/contact values; use it after any deploy that touches Sentry (README → "Confirming the Sentry pipeline"). Staging is **noindex** (`X-Robots-Tag` + `robots.txt` `Disallow: /`, staging builds only, `core/http/response-headers.ts`); production's indexability is still to be decided. `wrangler.jsonc` (Workers `maxoff-dev` / `maxoff-staging` / `maxoff`, `nodejs_compat`, compatibility date 2026-09-01), `open-next.config.ts` (no incremental cache), `.github/workflows/deploy.yml` (staging on `workflow_run` of a green CI on `main`; production on a `v*` tag that is on `main` with green CI, inert until a `production` GitHub environment exists with a required reviewer). **Phase tags are `phase-N-done`** (decided 2026-09-22): a tag named like its branch (`phase-0` is both) breaks plain `git push` / `git push --delete`, and `v*` tags trigger the production deploy. The existing `phase-0` tag stays as it is; from phase 1 on, `/review-phase` creates `phase-N-done`.
- **OpenNext does not build on this Windows machine (0.5):** `next build` passes, but `opennextjs-cloudflare build` fails with "Access is denied" on the pnpm symlinks it copies into `.open-next/server-functions`. WSL Ubuntu on this machine is broken too (`wsl -d Ubuntu` → `Wsl/Service/E_UNEXPECTED`), so `pnpm build:worker` and `pnpm preview` were **not** run locally; CI's `check` job (Ubuntu) runs `pnpm build:worker` and is the gate. Fixing WSL (or `wsl --update`) would also give a local preview.
- **Docker gotchas seen in 0.5 (both look like "Docker is down" and aren't):** (1) `docker` is not on PATH in a Git Bash / Claude shell opened before Docker Desktop was installed; `pnpm db:start` then fails with "docker: command not found". Prefix the command with `export PATH="$LOCALAPPDATA/Programs/DockerDesktop/resources/bin:$PATH"` or reopen the terminal. (2) After Docker Desktop has been up a while, the published port 54322 accepts TCP but Postgres never answers the handshake, so `pnpm db:test` fails with "Connection terminated unexpectedly" while `docker ps` says healthy. `docker restart supabase_db_maxoff` re-establishes the port forwarding; then `db:test` passes.
- **Phase 0 review fixes (2026-09-22):** production deploys only from a `main` commit whose three CI checks passed (`deploy.yml` gate); both deploy jobs build the Worker *before* `db push`; `core/errors` `action()` reports INTERNAL errors through `core/observability/capture` and logs only code + event id (the raw cause is printed only under `NODE_ENV=development`); `app/error.tsx` and `(app)/error.tsx` call `captureException`; `next.config.ts` `headers()` sets the security headers of ARCHITECTURE §18.3 and `e2e/production.spec.ts` asserts them. **A full CSP (script sources) is still open**; add it with the same spec when the inline-script set is final.
- **Sentry privacy rule (ARCHITECTURE §18.2):** `core/observability/scrub.ts` is `beforeSend` + `beforeBreadcrumb` on all three runtimes; `sendDefaultPii: false`; no request bodies, cookies or headers; user = `{ id }` only. `setSentryUser(id | null)` (`core/observability/user.ts`) is called by `core/auth` on every request that resolves a member and on sign-in/out, and `<SentryUser>` does the same in the browser from the app layout; `scrubEvent` drops `user.geo` with every other user field (unit-tested). New Sentry integrations must go through the scrubber. `NEXT_PUBLIC_APP_ENV` (`local | staging | production`) tags events; an empty DSN keeps the SDK off (local, CI); a **malformed** value fails `next build` (`assertObservabilityEnv()` in `next.config.ts`) and, if it ever reached runtime, degrades to "Sentry off" with a `console.error` instead of throwing in `instrumentation.ts`. **Query strings carry ids only** (ARCHITECTURE §18.2): the scrubber strips them, but Workers Logs don't. **3.x, when client names appear in clickable labels:** configure `breadcrumbsIntegration({ dom: { serializeAttribute: ["data-slot"] } })` so `ui.click` breadcrumbs stop serialising `aria-label` / `title`. **Before 5.1 adds push to `sw.js`:** the `maxoff-v1` cache keeps every deploy's `/_next/static` chunks with no eviction; key it on the build id or evict stale entries then.
- **`initOpenNextCloudflareForDev()` runs at the top of `next.config.ts`** (needed for `getCloudflareContext()` from 3.3). `next dev` and Playwright were fine with it. The Sentry wrapper (`withSentryConfig` from `@sentry/nextjs/config`) uploads source maps only when `SENTRY_AUTH_TOKEN` is set and never fails a build. The webpack-only options (`disableLogger`, `automaticVercelMonitors`) are deliberately absent: Turbopack ignores them and warns.
- **`wrangler types` is not wired (decided 0.5):** its generated `cloudflare-env.d.ts` includes Workers runtime globals that clash with the DOM lib (`document.head.append` typed as HTMLRewriter's `Element.append`, breaking `e2e/shell.spec.ts`). When 3.3 needs binding types (R2), generate with `wrangler types --include-runtime=false`, keep the file out of `tsconfig` `include` or add `@cloudflare/workers-types` deliberately, and add it to `.prettierignore` + ESLint `globalIgnores`.
- **`src/proxy.ts` is Node middleware and OpenNext 1.20.6 bundles it** (`bundleNodeMiddleware` in its build step; the CI Worker build proves the bundling). **Its runtime behaviour on the Worker is still to be confirmed on the first staging deploy after the phase-1 merge:** curl `/today` (expect 307 to `/login?next=%2Ftoday`) and `/login` (200). If the proxy ever fails there, the app is still safe (`requireMember()` in the layout is the decision; the proxy only refreshes cookies and redirects early), but sessions would stop refreshing on plain page loads.
- **Production guard (0.5), a must-keep for 1.2:** during 0.5 a curl against what looked like `next start` answered `/today` with 200 and `/` with 307. A fresh production build answers **404** for `/today`, `/my-day`, `/settings`, `/dev/ui` and every other shell route, ignores a preview-role cookie, and `/` is the landing page (200, no redirect). The 307 can only come from the preview-viewer redirect, which a production build never has, so the 200 came from a leftover `next dev` on port 3000: **reproduced** on 2026-09-22 (after `pnpm test:e2e`, a `next dev` still listened on 3000). Playwright's Windows child process outlives the run intermittently. Since 1.2 **every** Playwright project runs against `next start` on port **3100** of a fresh build (never a reused server): `e2e/production.spec.ts` proves every shell route answers 307 to `/login?next=`, `/` 307 to `/login`, no "Preview …" member or "UI gallery" text anywhere (the shims are deleted; the assertions stay forever), the old preview cookie opens nothing, security headers ride on the proxy redirects, and the service worker registers. Always check `netstat -ano | findstr :3000` before trusting a manual `next start` on this machine.
- **PWA (0.5):** `public/manifest.webmanifest`, `public/icons/` (PNGs rendered from `icon.svg` by `node scripts/generate-icons.mjs`, Playwright's Chromium), `public/sw.js` (offline shell only; push handlers arrive in 5.1: bump `VERSION` there), `public/_headers`, `/offline` page, `<RegisterServiceWorker />` registers only in production builds. `src/core/ui/pwa/pwa-files.test.ts` ties the manifest colours to `globals.css` tokens and the icon to `--logo`.
- **Quality gates (0.4):** `pnpm check` = typecheck + lint + format:check + unit + **pgTAP** + build, so Docker Desktop must be running and `pnpm db:start` done before `check`. **Playwright is outside `check` on purpose** (a minutes-long local gate gets skipped): `pnpm test:e2e` starts `next dev` itself; `/finish-task` runs it when a flow changed. CI (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`) = three jobs (`check`, `pgTAP` on a Postgres-only stack via `supabase start -x …`, `playwright`). **Branch protection on `main` is enabled** (verified 2026-09-22 during the phase 0 review: PR required, the three checks required and strict, no approving review count); the click path stays in the README for reference. CI sets placeholder `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_*` values so a future build-time env check passes; nothing in CI reaches a real database through them.
- **Playwright since 1.2:** `pnpm test:e2e` builds (`pnpm build && pnpm start --port 3100`, ~1.5 min all in) and needs the local Supabase stack **up and reset** (`pnpm db:start`, `pnpm db:reset`): the `setup` project signs in as the seeded Owner/Admin/Staff through the real form and saves `e2e/.auth/<role>.json` (git-ignored) for `desktop` / `mobile` (`test.use({ storageState: storageStateFor(role) })`, `e2e/helpers.ts`). `e2e/auth.spec.ts` covers wrong password, unknown email, the **deactivated user refused with the right password**, unsafe `next`, logout from the menu and from `/me`, the sign-in pages sending a member home, and the full recovery flow (forgot password → Mailpit API → `/auth/confirm` → set password with both validation errors → signed in → the link is one-time). That flow changes `reset@maxoff.local`'s password, so it has its own seed user and runs serially. In CI the Playwright job starts the stack (`-x realtime,storage-api,…`), writes `API_URL` / `PUBLISHABLE_KEY` from `supabase status -o env` into `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_*` **before** `pnpm build`, and stops it afterwards. Locators: form errors are `[data-slot="form-alert"]` and `[data-slot="field-error"]`, never `getByRole("alert")` (Next's route announcer is a second alert).
- **Lint enforces ARCHITECTURE §3.1 (0.4):** `eslint-plugin-boundaries` 7 (`boundaries/dependencies`, last matching policy wins) for app/core/module structure and for the database-client rule (`core/db` value imports only from `modules/*/data` and the core allow-list; type imports pass), `@typescript-eslint/no-restricted-imports` for `@supabase/*` outside those folders and for react/next/server-only inside `domain/`, `no-restricted-globals` for DOM globals in `domain/`, `no-restricted-syntax` for money relation literals outside `modules/revenue`. Element patterns are suffix-matched, so `tests/lint-fixtures/src/**` classifies like `src/**`; `tests/lint-rules.test.ts` asserts the exact rule ids per fixture. **Add a fixture pair whenever you add or loosen a rule.** The allow-lists (`DB_ALLOWED_CORE_AREAS`, `DOMAIN_ALLOWED_CORE_AREAS`, `MONEY_RELATIONS`) are arrays at the top of the config; a new money table in DATA-MODEL §7 goes into `MONEY_RELATIONS` in the same task. Files directly under `src/` (`proxy.ts`, `instrumentation.ts`) follow the app rules (`root` element), and `boundaries/no-unknown-files` fails on any `src/` file outside the known elements. `domain/` may **type**-import `core/db` (`Tables<>`, `Enums<>`), never value-import it. The money rule checks string and template literals only (word-bounded, so embedded selects like `"id, project_billing(amount)"` fail too); identifiers pass, and RLS is the real wall.
- **`core/realtime` is not in the database allow-list (decided 0.4).** `useRealtimeInvalidate()` (ARCHITECTURE §10) will need the browser client for `postgres_changes`; when 5.1 builds it, either add `realtime` to `DB_ALLOWED_CORE_AREAS` + CLAUDE.md rule 3 + ARCHITECTURE §3.1 + README in that task, or route the subscription through `core/db`. Decide then; don't widen the list silently.
- **Function grants (learned in 0.2):** Postgres grants EXECUTE to PUBLIC on every new function, and a per-schema `alter default privileges ... revoke` can't undo it. Every function in `app` and every future `security definer` function needs an explicit `revoke all on function ... from public` + `grant execute ... to authenticated, service_role`, and a pgTAP test that `anon` can't call it. The pgTAP grant tests caught this.
- **Decisions in 0.2 (2026-09-21):** Vitest arrived early so `core/time` and `core/errors` ship with tests; `pnpm test` is in `check`. `db:test` (pgTAP) joins `check` and CI in **0.4**. `.claude/settings.local.json` is git-ignored (personal hooks). ESLint forbids a bare `new Date()` outside `src/core/time/**` and tests (ADR-0008); use `todayIST()` / `systemClock`.
- **Error convention (ARCHITECTURE §4.3):** transition functions call `perform app.fail('INVALID_STATE', 'human reason')`; the message is the code, the detail is shown to the user. `core/errors` maps it; a `P0001` whose message isn't a known code becomes `INTERNAL`.
- **`core/db` entry points:** `@/core/db/server` (RLS, per request), `@/core/db/browser`, `@/core/db/service` (bypasses RLS, jobs only). The index exports types and env readers only, so `server-only` never reaches a browser bundle. Env names: `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL`, `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY`, `SUPABASE_SECRET_KEY` (legacy anon / service_role JWTs also work).
- `src/core/db/database.types.ts` is generated by `pnpm db:types`; rerun it after every migration. `@types/node` is now ^24 (Vitest 5 peer).
- **UI screenshots live locally in `screenshots/<date>-<task>/` (git-ignored).** Capture with `node .impeccable/shots.mjs <label>` while `pnpm dev` runs (`.impeccable/` is git-ignored too; the script needs Playwright's Chromium, already installed). Keep one set per UI task so before/after can be compared.
- **The 0.3 shims are gone (1.2):** the preview-role cookie and switcher and the `/dev/ui` gallery were deleted; `e2e/production.spec.ts` keeps asserting they never come back. The composites are now verified through the real screens and the Playwright specs; `.impeccable/shots.mjs` (local, git-ignored) needs a signed-in session if it is used again.
- **1.2 decisions (2026-09-22), all in ADR-0012, ARCHITECTURE §7a, DATA-MODEL §0a/§1, WORKFLOWS §1:** login/logout write **only** `session_events` (no `activity_log` row); `ip_hash` = SHA-256 of `salt:ip` with **`SESSION_IP_HASH_SALT`** (new secret, null when unset; add it to the `staging` GitHub environment, and the workflow uploads it as a Worker secret in a step that is skipped while the secret is missing, so a deploy never fails over it); logout is **this device only** (`signOut({ scope: "local" })`); passwords **≥ 12 characters, no composition rule**, plus leaked-password protection on hosted projects; auth links use **`/auth/confirm?token_hash=…&type=…`** (`verifyOtp`, no PKCE), and `supabase/templates/recovery.html` is the text the hosted "Reset password" template must carry; `/forgot-password` exists (same route as the bootstrap link); **hosted auth settings are a README checklist** (sign-ups off, password rules, Site URL + redirect URLs, template, SMTP, rate limits), not `supabase config push`, so the Resend key never enters CI. **`pnpm bootstrap:owner -- --email … --name … [--org …]`** creates the auth user without a password, calls `bootstrap_owner()` (service role only, `CONFLICT` once any member exists, rolls the auth user back on failure) and prints a one-time recovery link; verified locally (refusal on a live team, creation on an empty one, the link opens `/set-password` once and `/login?reason=link` the second time, an empty or guessed password is refused at GoTrue). Local `config.toml`: `[auth] enable_signup = false`, `minimum_password_length = 12`, `[auth.rate_limit] email_sent = 100` (Mailpit only), recovery template wired. **Gotchas:** `[auth.email] enable_signup = false` turns off email *logins* (it is the provider switch), so it stays `true`; a `config.toml` change needs `pnpm db:stop && pnpm db:start` (`db:reset` doesn't reload Auth); Supabase's default privileges grant EXECUTE on new `public` functions to **`anon`**, so every function revokes `from public, anon` explicitly (the pgTAP grant tests caught it); both pgTAP files now **delete the seed rows first** (inside their transaction) because `supabase test db` runs on the seeded database and the single-Owner index would otherwise reject the fixtures; the seed inserts `auth.users` + `auth.identities` rows with `crypt(password, gen_salt('bf'))`.
- **No sending domain yet (owner, 2026-09-22):** Resend has no verified domain, so **a verified `mail.maxoff.app` is required before 1.3 sends real invites and before 5.2 sends notification email.** Until then: local auth mail lands in Mailpit (port 54324); staging auth mail (recovery links) uses Supabase's built-in mailer, which delivers only to the Supabase project's own team addresses, a few per hour; the first Owner needs no mail at all (printed link). The app-side seam is `core/notifications` `sendEmail()` (`email.ts`: Resend through `fetch`, or a log sender when `RESEND_API_KEY` is unset that logs the intended recipient + subject in development and only the subject elsewhere, never throws, answers `not_configured`). `instrumentation.ts` logs one start-up line when the key is missing (`console.warn`, `console.error` in production, never a crash). **5.4 (reachability screen): a missing `RESEND_API_KEY` must surface there as an operational warning to the Owner**, not only as a server log. `RESEND_API_KEY` / `EMAIL_FROM` are in `.env.example` and stay out of CI. **Do not enable a verified sending domain until link prefetch is handled** (ROADMAP 5.2 prerequisite, phase 1 review): `/auth/confirm` verifies on GET today, and a mail scanner's prefetch would spend every invite and recovery link.
- **1.3 decisions (2026-09-22/23, owner-approved), in WORKFLOWS §1a, DATA-MODEL §0a/§1/§2, ADR-0012 amendment, README "Inviting people":** invites are **app-sent**: `auth.admin.generateLink({ type: "invite" })` (service client, Auth admin API only) creates the sign-in without a password and returns the token hash; rpc `member_invite()` writes the `invited` row (auth user deleted again if it fails); the link `/auth/confirm?token_hash=…&type=invite` is built on `NEXT_PUBLIC_APP_URL` (`core/lib/app-url.ts`, request host as the local fallback) and mailed through `sendEmail()` (log sender until Resend has a domain, and the dialog says so). **There is no GoTrue invite template.** "Copy invite link" is **Owner-only** (`team.manage`): rpc `member_invite_refresh()` (audited `invite_link_issued`) then `generateLink` again; **the previous link stops working** (GoTrue keeps one confirmation token per user; proven by probe and by `e2e/team.spec.ts`). Links are one-time and live **24 h** (`otp_expiry = 86400` locally and in the hosted checklist; it governs recovery links too: **10.3 must revisit**). **RLS hides a member row from anyone not active**, so the link and set-password steps read the caller's status through `member_self_status()` (security definer, own row only); `verifyAuthLink()` admits `invited` for an invite **or** recovery link (opening any link confirms the sign-in at GoTrue even without a password; from then on `generateLink` invite answers `email_exists`, so `issueInviteToken()` falls back to a `recovery` token and the link carries `type=recovery`; "Forgot password" recovers an abandoned invite), `setPassword()` then runs `member_accept_invite()` + `session_login()` and lands on `/me?welcome=1`. The invite action checks `members` by email **before** issuing any token (a second invite would otherwise rotate the pending person's link). `NEXT_PUBLIC_APP_URL` missing on staging/production makes the invite action fail loud (`resolveAppOrigin`), never a Host-header link. **Revoke invite = `member_deactivate()` from `invited`** (worded by state); reactivating someone who never joined returns them to `invited`. Deactivation records an **optional reason** in `activity_log.meta.reason` and **deletes the person's `auth.refresh_tokens` and `auth.sessions` in the same transaction** (pgTAP proves only the target's rows go; Playwright replays a captured refresh token at GoTrue: accepted before, `refresh_token_not_found` after). The `postgres` role's DELETE on the `auth` tables is verified locally; **check once on staging after the phase-1 merge** (deactivate a test member, confirm their refresh fails). Email change is **deferred to 1.4** (ROADMAP). Every team transition writes its audit row through **`app.audit_override`** (a transaction setting the trigger consumes: action name + meta, one row, no generic 'update' duplicate): use the same in every future transition function on an audited table. Job titles: `list_items` seeded per organization by trigger (`app.seed_org_lists()`), backfilled for the existing org; `core/lists` = `index.ts` (registry + schemas, client-safe) and `server.ts` (repository; `createListItem` appends with `nextPosition()`; 1.4 builds the Settings screen on it). `members.job_title_id` is in the API UPDATE grant; the self-edit guard keeps it `team.manage`-only and `app.members_job_title_guard()` refuses archived/foreign titles. `<Can role permission>` lives in `core/permissions`. 5.4 still has to delete push subscriptions on deactivation.
- **1.4 decisions (2026-09-23, owner-approved), in DATA-MODEL §0a/§1, WORKFLOWS §1a/§9, PERMISSIONS §3, ARCHITECTURE §3.2/§7:** `holidays` is the **one configuration table with a real DELETE** (it has no `archived_at`): removing a mistyped date is the Owner's, and `audit_row_change()` keeps the removed row. **2.1 must keep that harmless:** `attendance_days` carries its own `is_day_off`, decided on the day itself, so deleting a holiday later must never re-read a past day — never derive a past day's meaning from today's `holidays` / `weekly_off_days`, only from the stored row. `app.is_working_day(date)` is `stable` + `security definer` (a service-role job reads no `org_settings` row under RLS and would call every day a working day); `core/time` `isWorkingDay(date, {weeklyOffDays, holidays})` is its pure mirror. **Email change** (`member_change_email()`, the `changeMemberEmail` action): active, invited or the **Owner's own** row; sessions stay alive (nothing reads the email from the JWT — `getSessionState()` reads `members`); **both addresses are emailed** (the old one is the message that matters when the mailbox is still live) and both bypass the daily cap. Thresholds validate in zod only (Owner escalation ≥ Admin escalation; all seven days off refused), so the table's own checks stay as they are. **A pending invite link dies when the address moves** (proved by probe against the local stack: `verifyOtp` answers `otp_expired` after `updateUserById`), so the dialog tells the Owner to send a fresh one with "Copy invite link", and `e2e/team.spec.ts` holds that behaviour in place — the first draft of the docs and the dialog claimed the opposite. Job titles are `lists.manage`, so **Admins edit them too** (PERMISSIONS §1 wins over the roadmap's wording). Reordering is a **position swap** through one `upsert` of both rows (Move up / Move down), not a midpoint key: no half-written order, and it works on a phone and from a keyboard. `SETTINGS_SECTIONS` in `core/ui/shell/nav.ts` now carries `description` + `ready`, so a section that is not built yet stays a card instead of linking into an empty page — **set `ready: true` in the task that builds it** (3.2 custom fields, 4.1 task types, 4.6 templates, 7.4 stage presets, 8.3 Drive).
- **Phase 1 review (2026-09-23, architecture-reviewer + security review of `main...phase-1`): no must-fix; fixed before the merge:** (1) the `sb-*` session cookies were `httpOnly: false` (the `@supabase/ssr` default, there for a browser client MaxOff never uses), so any script on a page could read the refresh token; `core/db/cookies.ts` now passes `httpOnly`, `SameSite=Lax` and `Secure` (staging/production) to both server clients, and `core/db/browser.ts` documents that it carries no session (5.1 Realtime: `realtime.setAuth()` from a server prop); (2) `updateListItem()` nulled `description`/`color`/`icon` on a name-only rename (`listItemUpdatePatch()` writes only the keys present); (3) DATA-MODEL §9 still described the dropped "own actions" activity-log clause; (4) the bootstrap script and README said a link expires in an hour (it is 24 h). **Should-fix items awaiting the Owner's decision (not blocking the merge):** (a) `setPassword` admits any active session, not only one a link opened, so a stolen session can set a permanent password — **deferred to 10.3 by the Owner** (gate it on a recent `otp` `amr` claim); the cheap mitigation was tested first: `secure_password_change = true` (config.toml + hosted checklist) kept every Playwright flow green (81 passed; invite-accept and recovery links open sessions seconds old), but a probe proved GoTrue asks for the `reauthenticate()` nonce **only when the session is older than 24 h** (a fresh password sign-in changed the password with no nonce), so it narrows the window without closing it; (b) a reactivated person can read the Owner's deactivation reason through the activity-log self clause (`entity = 'members' and entity_id = self`): exclude `action = 'deactivated'` from that clause or warn the Owner in the dialog, then document in PERMISSIONS §2; (c) column-level UPDATE grants exist only on `members`: an Admin with `lists.manage` can set `list_items.list_key`, `position`, `is_system`, `meta` and `created_at` through PostgREST, and the Owner can set `organizations.timezone` (invariant 8 assumes IST) — next migration: `revoke update on <table> from authenticated` + `grant update (editable columns)` on `organizations`, `org_settings`, `list_items`, `holidays`, with `column_privs_are` pgTAP tests, and the rule written into ARCHITECTURE §4.1; (d) `/auth/confirm` verifies the one-time token on the GET itself, so a mail scanner (Defender SafeLinks, Gmail, a chat unfurler) that prefetches the link spends it before the person clicks — must become a "Continue" page whose POST verifies, **before the Resend sending domain goes live** (1.3 invites and 5.2 email); `e2e/helpers.ts` `confirmLinkFrom()` then submits that form.
- **Architecture review of the 1.4 diff (2026-09-23) found six things, all fixed before the commit** (migration `20260923021533_settings_list_move.sql`): (1) the Owner's **DELETE on `holidays` was only ever exercised as `postgres`**, so the policy the "real delete" rests on had no allowed-path test, and Staff UPDATE/DELETE denial was untested — both are in `04` now; (2) `changeMemberEmail` moved the **GoTrue sign-in before checking the target's state**, so a stale tab could move a deactivated person's sign-in and then fail the rpc — the action now mirrors every rpc precondition **before** touching the un-rollbackable side; (3) the "new address" notice told an **invited** person to use "your existing password", which they do not have — the copy branches on `accepted`; (4) `moveListItem` wrote **whole rows** back, so a reorder silently reverted a concurrent rename or archive and the audit log made it look deliberate — it is now `list_item_move()`, one UPDATE of two `position` values (pgTAP asserts the audit diff holds `position` alone); (5) **`app.is_working_day()` answered `true` for every date when `app.current_org_id()` is null**, which would have marked a whole team absent on a Sunday in 2.5 — it answers `null` now, and callers must treat null as "do not act"; (6) `findMemberByEmail` used `ilike`, where `_` and `%` are wildcards and both are legal in an email — now an exact match on the lower-cased address. Still open by choice: `app.is_working_day()` is callable by any authenticated user, so an invited or deactivated person could enumerate the holiday list (near-zero sensitivity, and phase 2's service-role callers have no member row); `list_items.position` has no per-list unique index, so two simultaneous swaps of overlapping pairs could still tie.
- **pgTAP now survives a Playwright run.** `01` asserts the launch defaults on a **freshly inserted organization** instead of the seeded one (Settings can edit the seeded one), `03` and `05` put the job-title list back to the seed in their fixtures, and `03`'s reactivation audit lookup orders by `id`, not `at`. Before this, `pnpm check` failed after `pnpm test:e2e` until you ran `pnpm db:reset`. **Two pgTAP traps worth remembering:** a test inside a `savepoint` that is rolled back still prints but is **not counted by `finish()`** (pgTAP keeps its results in the transaction), so the plan and the runner disagree by one — put such a test last and let the file's own rollback clean up; and `array_agg(jsonb_object_keys(...))` is a set-returning call inside an aggregate, which Postgres refuses — use `lateral`.
- **`members.email` and `auth.users.email` are two systems (1.4).** The action moves the sign-in at GoTrue first (`auth.admin.updateUserById`, `email_confirm: true`), then calls `member_change_email()`, which **asserts `auth.users.email` already matches** and puts the sign-in back if it refuses. The impossible direction is therefore "MaxOff shows an address the sign-in does not have", which would lock someone out; the possible one is a crash between the two calls leaving the sign-in moved and the member row behind, so the person signs in with the new address while MaxOff shows the old. `restoreAuthEmail()` logs that case (code only). **A reconciliation check belongs in 6.6 (backups / ops) or the 10.3 hardening pass:** a small query comparing `members.email` with `auth.users.email` per member, surfaced to the Owner, since nothing today would notice the drift.
- **GitHub workflow edits must be validated (2026-09-22):** `secrets` is not available in a step-level `if` (deploy.yml failed to parse and no deploy could run; fixed in `150989b` by checking `-z "$SESSION_IP_HASH_SALT"` inside the step). After any workflow change, open the Actions tab on the next push: "Invalid workflow file" is the only signal. Audit of both workflows on 2026-09-22 found no other misplaced context.
- **Playwright and emailed / built links (2026-09-22/23):** GoTrue builds recovery links from `config.toml` `site_url` (port 3000) and the app builds invite links from `.env.local` `NEXT_PUBLIC_APP_URL` (also 3000), while the suite runs on **3100**. Always pass such links through `e2e/helpers.ts` `confirmLinkFrom()` / `onBaseURL()` (path + query re-based on `baseURL`; asserts `/auth/confirm` and `token_hash`). A stray `next start` on 3000 masked this locally and CI run 35732609637 was red until `dc415c0`. Also: **`browser.newContext()` inherits the test's `storageState`**; a tab that must start signed out needs `{ storageState: { cookies: [], origins: [] } }`. `playwright.config.ts` loads `.env.local` (`process.loadEnvFile`) so specs can call GoTrue directly. **CI's Playwright job must export every runtime variable `next start` needs from `supabase status -o env`:** since 1.3 that includes `SUPABASE_SECRET_KEY` (the invite action's service client); its absence showed up only as `[action] INTERNAL` in the WebServer log (CI run 35801459776). `e2e/team.spec.ts` needs `pnpm db:reset` before every run (it creates `invitee@` / `pending@maxoff.local`) and asserts the "Email is not set up yet" copy, so a `RESEND_API_KEY` in `.env.local` breaks it locally. **The recovery flow is known-flaky and is ROADMAP 2.6, not folklore:** it takes the newest Mailpit message for `reset@maxoff.local` after a global `clearMailbox()`, so a message still in flight from an earlier run can hand it a link that was already spent (it then lands on `/login?reason=link`). It failed once in 1.5 and passed in isolation and on three later full runs. **Do not re-run and move on — fix it in 2.6.** Until then, `curl -s -X DELETE http://127.0.0.1:54324/api/v1/messages` before `pnpm test:e2e` makes it reliable.
- **React/Next gotchas from 1.3:** a `useTransition` that awaits a server action stays **pending until Next's route refresh** after `revalidatePath` completes, so a dialog whose close is gated on `pending` may refuse to close after success (the invite dialog gates only the form phase). `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` forbids a synchronous `setState` in an effect: mount a dialog fresh per subject (`key={member.id}`) instead of resetting state in an effect. A pgTAP transaction shares one `now()`: order `activity_log` lookups by `id`, never by `at`. `WITH … UPDATE … RETURNING` cannot sit inside a subquery ("data-modifying statement must be at the top level"). Seeded `list_items` reference the organization, so a test that empties `organizations` deletes `list_items` first (audit trigger paused). **pgTAP cleanups delete `activity_log` before `members`** (`actor_id` FK): after a Playwright run the log holds rows by the seeded Owner, and the old order made `pnpm check` fail locally while CI (fresh stack) stayed green.
- **Versions installed by 0.1 (2026-09-21):** Next **16.3.5** (Turbopack), React **19.2.8**, TypeScript **6.0.3**, Tailwind **4.3.3** (`@tailwindcss/postcss`), ESLint **9.39.5** + `eslint-config-next` 16.3.5, Prettier **3.9.8** (+ `prettier-plugin-tailwindcss` 0.8.1, `eslint-config-prettier` 10.1.8), Node 24.14.1, pnpm 12.5.1. **No fallback was needed:** everything is `create-next-app`'s own combination except TypeScript, which is pinned to 6.0.3 because `eslint-config-next` bundles `typescript-eslint@^8`, whose peer range is `typescript <6.1.0` — TypeScript 7.0.2 (the native port) would break type-aware lint. `typecheck`, `lint` and `build` all pass on 6.0.3. Prefer the scaffold's working combination over the newest release when upgrading.
- **No stub scripts:** a command is in `check` only once it really runs (CLAUDE.md §Commands says the same).
- **Prettier owns code only.** `*.md` is in `.prettierignore` so the hand-formatted docs, ADRs and `CLAUDE.md` stay byte-stable.
- **`agentRules: false` in `next.config.ts` is deliberate.** `next dev` otherwise appends a `nextjs-agent-rules` block to `CLAUDE.md` on every run. Next 16's own guidance is readable at `node_modules/next/dist/docs/` — worth a look, since Next 16 differs from model training data.
- **Turbopack + OpenNext (verified 0.5):** `@opennextjs/cloudflare` 1.20.6 lists Turbopack builds as supported for every Next 16 minor, so the `build` script stays `next build`. CI confirms it on Ubuntu (see the Windows note above).
- **shadcn/ui v4 (0.3):** CLI `shadcn@4.21` with the **Radix** base (`radix-nova` style), Tailwind v4, React 19. `components.json` aliases put primitives in `src/core/ui/primitives` and `cn()` in `src/core/lib/utils` (a re-export of the `cn` package; `clsx`/`tailwind-merge` aren't used). Add a primitive with `pnpm dlx shadcn@4.21.0 add <name> -y`, then `pnpm format` + `pnpm lint:fix`; strict TS (`exactOptionalPropertyTypes`) may need a one-line fix (see `dropdown-menu.tsx`, `sonner.tsx`). Never run `shadcn init` in the repo root: it rewrites `globals.css`. The `shadcn` package is a **runtime** dependency because `globals.css` imports `shadcn/tailwind.css` (its data-* variants). TanStack Table is pinned to **v8** (`^8.21.3`); v9 is an API rewrite.
- **Design tokens are law (ARCHITECTURE §3.3):** every colour comes from `globals.css`; the six `StatusBadge` tones and their pill backgrounds are tested for 4.5:1 by reading the CSS. Light attention is `#AD5009` (the brief's `#B45309` fails 4.5:1 on a 12% tint). The `logo` token is fixed red in both themes; `brand` lightens in dark. Info (blue) is only in progress / submitted / converted.
- **Server → client boundary gotcha (0.3):** a Lucide icon component in a plain object crossed from a server component into a client one and every shell route 500'd while `next build` stayed green. Nav items carry an icon *name*; `shell/nav-icons.ts` resolves it in client code. Smoke-test routes in the browser or with curl after touching the shell: the build alone doesn't catch this.
- **Next dev-tools button** is moved to bottom-right (`devIndicators.position` in `next.config.ts`) so it doesn't cover the sidebar footer. In development on a phone it overlaps the Staff "Me" tab; production has no button.
- **`forbidden()` / `unauthorized()` are still experimental in Next 16.3** (`authInterrupts`). 0.3 uses a `/forbidden` route + `<Forbidden />` composite instead; `requirePermission` (1.1) redirects there.
- The repo root is this folder. `CLAUDE.md`, `docs/`, `.claude/`, `BUILD-GUIDE.md`, `.gitattributes` and `.gitignore` already exist and must never be overwritten by a generator. (0.1 scaffolded into a temp folder and copied in selectively; the scaffold's own `CLAUDE.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `README.md` and `.gitignore` were discarded.)
- Windows machine: Node 24, pnpm 12.5.1 and `gh` installed; long paths and PowerShell RemoteSigned are set; `git core.longpaths=true`. The Supabase CLI is a dev dependency (`pnpm supabase`). Docker Desktop (per-user install, `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Programs\DockerDesktop`) was installed on 2026-09-21; a terminal opened before the install won't have `docker` on PATH until it's reopened. Docker Desktop must be running for `db:*` commands; `.env.local` is still to be filled from `pnpm db:status` (needed from 1.2).
- The docs were rewritten for **v2**. `docs/research/` holds the source material (v2 spec, clarifications, Kyran research) and is background only. **`docs/PRODUCT.md` wins where they differ.**

## Ideas / tech debt (don't build these without adding them to the roadmap)

- **5.1 step 1, later items (2026-09-30, the 5A builder's own review of `12bc0ca`):** (a) **`end_day_reminder` runs every 5 minutes through the IST evening** (`*/5 12-18 * * *`, ~84 `cron.job_run_details` rows a day) so a changed `logout_reminder_time` is honoured; when 5B adds `reminders_tick` (every 5 min, WORKFLOWS §8) fold the reminder into it and drop the separate cron row (expand-only: `cron.unschedule('end_day_reminder')` in that migration), and give `cron.job_run_details` a retention job. (b) **`notification_deliveries` email rows and the quiet-hours hold** are the dispatcher's (steps 3–4); `app.notify()` never decides email, on purpose: a kind's `actionable` / `always_email` flags are read at dispatch, so a later flag change applies to rows already queued. (c) **A coordinator's rows carry " · for <name>" on the title** (`app.notify`); when step 2 builds the list, the freelancer's name may deserve its own line from `payload.for_member_id` instead of the title suffix (the push title stays as it is). (d) **`coordinator_missing` is written with no actor** (only the Owner manages the team, decision 17): the one row where the "never the actor" rule yields; if team.manage ever reaches Admins, restore the actor there. (e) **No e2e checks a notification row yet**: step 2's bell and history specs are the first readers; until then the rows are proved by pgTAP `40` only.
- **Phase 4 review, later items (2026-09-30, `/review-phase 4`: the architecture (A-) and security (S-) reviews of `origin/main...phase-4` on `3cf15f2`; every must-fix and should-fix is fixed in `f50cbd2`…`4c3b8b1` and the parked remainder, see "Phase 4 review fixes" under Current state):**
  - (A-L1) **The mark-read race:** `TaskChat` records `marked.current = newestAt` and clears the tab's count (`markSeen`) before `task_mark_read` answers (`task-chat.tsx`), so a failed or slow mark is not retried in that visit and the lists keep the marker until the next visit; each mark also revalidates `/tasks` as a layout, re-rendering the open task page. Retry once on failure, or mark from the server when the thread renders.
  - (A-L2) **`?tab=chat` is left in the address on a phone:** a phone that arrives on `?tab=chat` opens the sheet once over Work (`TaskViewProvider`), but closing the sheet leaves `?tab=chat`, so a refresh (or pull-to-refresh) opens the sheet again. Replace the query with Work when the sheet closes.
  - (A-L3) **The task page's loading screen guesses the next step by role** (`tasks/[id]/loading.tsx`): Staff always get a next-step bar and managers none, so a Staff member on a completed task, or an Admin with a review step, sees the bar appear or vanish when the data lands. Trace it from something the loading screen can know, or reserve the bar for both.
  - (A-L4) **`modules/team` imports `modules/tasks`** (`team/actions/members.ts`: `countOpenAssignments`, for the deactivation warning): module to module through `index.ts`, which the boundaries rule allows, but it couples the two; move the count behind a core read or pass it in from `app/`.
  - (A-L5) **A `data-scroll-x` sweep:** the marker exempts a bar from `expectNoHorizontalScroll` (`e2e/helpers.ts`; the task page's views bar, decision 32, is the one designed case). Sweep the other sideways scrollers (the core `Table`'s `overflow-x-auto` wrapper) and decide each: a designed exception (mark it and say so in ARCHITECTURE §14.1) or a fault the check must keep catching; the marker must never become a way to make a large-text check pass.
  - (A-L6) **`/today` is at 780.1 KB of its 785 KB first-load budget** (`bundle-budget.json`): the next feature on `/today` breaks `pnpm budget`. Find what the route pulls in before phase 5 adds to it.
  - (S-L1) **Check the hosted projects' Data API "Exposed schemas"** (staging and production): `app` must not be listed (only `public` and `graphql_public`), since `app.*` helpers are granted to `authenticated` for policies; a dashboard check, owner-side.
  - (S-L3) **Money in a task custom field:** nothing stops an Owner or Admin from defining a task custom field that holds an amount, which Staff on the task would read (invariant 2). Decide whether custom fields on tasks refuse a currency kind, or document it in PRODUCT as the Owner's responsibility.
  - (S-L6) **`member_directory` shows `status` and `engagement`** to everyone who can read a row (Staff read the people on their tasks, decision 21): harmless today (names only on screen), but trim the columns Staff do not need when the view is next recreated.
  - (S-L7) **The e2e hold proxy relays to whatever `HOLD_PROXY_UPSTREAM` names** (`e2e/hold-proxy.ts`): it listens on 127.0.0.1 only, but it should also refuse an upstream that is not loopback, so a misconfigured run can never put a remote database behind it.

- **4C, later items (2026-09-30, the second 4C builder):** (a) **The task types dialog asks nothing on back** with a typed name (§14.2 f), unlike 4C's Suggest and template dialogs and the task dialog: give it the same "Discard?" pattern (4C mechanics (4)) if the owner wants every form alike. (b) **The list managers' Restore** (task types, templates, the 1.4 list manager, custom fields) is a ghost button with a busy flag, not `useAction`: no working label, not disabled offline, no "Couldn't reach MaxOff" (PR #22's sweep covers only `primary` / `submit`). (c) **An Admin may decide their own suggestion** (the SQL's rule: they see it and hold `task_requests.decide`), and it counts in their "suggested tasks to decide"; if the owner wants someone else to decide an Admin's suggestion, that is a new rule in `task_request_convert` / `_decline` (an owner question for `/review-phase 4`). (d) Suggested tasks keeps the latest 50 decided ones and no more (no paging); a withdrawn one shows no date (`decided_at` is only for converted / declined). (e) ~~**The held-skeleton check of an Admin's `/today`** lost its race once in 20 loads~~ **done** (4C review M1: held by its data, `e2e/loading-screens.spec.ts`). (f) **The Owner's team month at large text (3b.4, not 4C):** a row whose unpaid expense line is long ("₹1,200.50 to pay · 1 claim") reaches past a 375/430 phone at 130% and 200%, because its trailing part is `CARD_ROW_TRAILING` (`shrink-0`) and cannot wrap; `mobile.spec`'s `/reports/month` check goes red whenever `expenses.spec` approved its claim first (order-dependent; reproduced on `e8764c2`). Let the trailing part wrap (`min-w-0`, the amount on its own line) in `month-summary.tsx`. (g) ~~**The held-skeleton checks of fast pages race**~~ **done** (4C review M1: the page's data is held at the e2e server's Supabase proxy, so no page answers before its shell; `e2e/loading-screens.spec.ts`). (h) **The 30 s test timeout and this container:** `expenses.spec:69` and `extra-work.spec:58` take 27–32 s here (the same on the pre-4C commit), so a full local run reds on them; CI's clean runner is the proof (2.6: never raise a timeout). (i) **`data-slot` overrides on modals** (4B's task dialogs `task-form-dialog`, `task-done-dialog`, `task-comment-dialog`, `task-stage-dialog`, `task-approver-dialog`; 4C's ⋯ sheets `task-type-actions`, `task-template-actions`) replace the primitive's slot and so drop `[data-slot$="dialog-content"]` / `sheet-content`'s overscroll containment; the specs could find them by role or a `data-testid`-like attribute instead.
- **4C later items (review) (2026-09-30, the 4C fixer; L1, L2 and L6 are fixed in `phase4c_review_fixes` and `domain/lists.ts`):** (L3) **the task types and template managers' `run()`** (`task-types-manager.tsx:76-80`, the template manager alike) does not catch: a network failure leaves `busyId` set (the row stays busy), shows no pending label and nothing disables it offline; give it `useAction`'s shape (Ideas "4C, later items" (b)). (L4) **`coordinator-dialogs.tsx:54` has no `.catch`**: offline, `getCoordinatorChoices` never settles and the dialog says "Loading…" forever; the same for the handover choices and the open-task count read behind Invite as employee: catch and show "Couldn't reach MaxOff" with a retry. (L5) **`modules/team` imports `modules/tasks`** (`src/modules/team/actions/members.ts:15`, `countOpenAssignments` for Invite as employee's warning): a module reaching into another's index; compose it in `app/` (the page reads the count and hands it to the dialog) instead. (L7) **Unindexed sorts:** `listFinishedTaskRows` (`order by updated_at desc limit 300` over completed / cancelled tasks) and the decided-requests read (`neq state pending order by updated_at desc limit 50`) have no index on the sort; run EXPLAIN on `measure-seed` volumes when the tables grow (ARCHITECTURE §19: an index only with an EXPLAIN that uses it). (L8) **`/today` and `/my-day` stand at 783.5 of 785 KB**: 1.5 KB left for 5.1's bell and Realtime; plan what leaves the shell first. (L9) **Form dialogs that lose typed work on back** against §14.2 f: the task types dialog (a name and a kind), People's Add person, Change coordinator's reason, Invite as employee's email; give them the "Discard?" pattern (4C mechanics (4)). (L10) **Offer a reassign before Invite as employee:** its warning counts the person's open tasks nobody can act on until they accept; a "Reassign them first" link to the filtered full list would close the loop (an owner question).
- **`main` merge, later items (2026-09-29):** (a) **A prefetch the proxy redirects leaves a stale redirect behind:** the fetch follows the proxy's 307s without the `_rsc` marker, Next answers the target with its cache-busting 307 (`/my-day` → `/my-day?_rsc=…`, no `Cache-Control`, no `Vary`), the browser keeps it under the page's address, and Chrome replays it on a back/forward load of that address (the app lands on `?_rsc=`). The sign-in page's Forgot link, re-prefetched with the new session, was the one trigger in the suite (no longer prefetched, `63371f9`); the error screens' "Go home" (`<Link href="/">`, `src/app/error.tsx`, `src/app/(app)/error.tsx`) is another (`/` → home): `prefetch={false}` there too, or raise it with Next (that 307 should carry `Vary` or `no-store`). (b) **`/people` starts `listDirectory()` before the role is known** (PR #22, §19): only the Owner and Admins pass its check, but a Staff member opening `/people` by address starts the 2 s whole-directory read ("4B mechanics" (1)) before the redirect; start it behind the check, or rewrite `app.directory_visible()` set-based (4C). (c) `e2e/pull-to-refresh.spec.ts` now runs serially (its tests share one seeded person per project; `0c52d2a`).

- **4B, "later" items (2026-09-29):** (a) **`app.directory_visible()` is slow for anyone without `team.view`** (4A later item (a), now measured: 2 s for 82 members and 33 tasks for one Staff read of `member_directory`); 4B reads by ids (4B mechanics (1)); rewrite it as one join on the caller's own assignee, creator, approver and coordinator rows (a migration with pgTAP, expand-only: same signature and meaning) before a Staff screen needs the whole list. (b) The task page starts the edit dialog's setup (six reads) for every manager's view, though few open Edit: move it behind the first open (a server action) if the page's reads ever queue. (c) Settings → Task types (the Owner's editor with `task_type_move()`) and per-type task fields in Settings → Custom fields are not built (4C or with 5.3's reminder editor; "Last completed: unit 4B"). (d) A stage cannot be renamed from the task page (remove and add); the guard allows a manager's rename, a small dialog if the pilot asks. (e) `MODAL_SURFACE`'s `duration-200` without a `transition-property` transitions every property (the padding eases after a text-size change, 4B mechanics (4)); name the animated properties if a real screen ever shows it. (f) The history lists the latest 50 entries (`ACTIVITY_LIMIT`) with no "show earlier"; a busy task may need paging. (g) A stage tick's audit row carries no stage id or name (4B mechanics (6)); if the history must always name it, `app.task_stages_guard()` could set an audit override with the stage's name on the API path (a migration).

- **4A later items (review)** (2026-09-29, the orchestrator's independent review of 4A; M1, M2, S1–S4 are fixed in `20260929104507_phase4a_review_fixes`):
